

================================================================================
## Core monitoring and display  [monitoring-display]
================================================================================

### iOS REQUIREMENTS (58)
- The app must present a single scrolling Home dashboard whose card order is fixed and identical to Android: connection/sync status row, glucose hero, recent-meal glance (only when a recent meal exists), glucose trend chart, Time in Range, CGM Stats, Insulin Summary, Recent Boluses, then driver-contributed cards in ascending priority order, then a single footer line ('Pair your pump in Settings to start' when disconnected; 'Loading pump data...' when connected with no CGM value; nothing otherwise).
- Every dashboard card must render even with no data, each showing its own empty-state message; no card may be hidden for lack of data.
- The user must be able to pull down on Home to force an immediate refresh. A refresh must read pump values over BLE sequentially in the order IoB, basal rate, battery, reservoir, CGM with a 500 ms gap between reads, must route the CGM result through the same path the background poller uses so a manual refresh can feed the alert floor immediately, and must concurrently reconcile glucose range, glucose unit, meal-intelligence, analytics config, pump profile, safety limits and alert thresholds when a backend is configured. A refresh requested while one is in flight must be dropped, not queued.
- Every time the app returns to the foreground, it must immediately re-run the pump read sequence, because iOS suspends the app in the background and the last value may be arbitrarily old.
- Any reconciliation or refresh failure must be logged and must leave the previous cached values intact; no failure may blank or reset a displayed value.
- The status row must show a pump-link indicator in every mode with six distinct states (connected, connecting/authenticating, reconnecting, scanning, pairing failed, disconnected), using the Android colour semantics (blue = connected, amber = transitional, red = failed or absent) and the same accompanying text, including showing NO text for the connected and disconnected resting states. The crossed-out state must be presented as a resting state, not an error.
- The sync and backend-reachability indicators must be hidden entirely when no backend is configured, and in their healthy states must remain visually distinguishable from each other (a transfer/up-down glyph for sync, a cloud-with-check for reachability), so a user can tell 'my data is uploaded' from 'my server answers right now'.
- Backend reachability must be derived only from traffic the app already makes: any HTTP response, including 4xx and 5xx, proves reachability; only two consecutive transport-level failures flip to unreachable. No health-check endpoint may be added. Device-offline must take precedence over backend-unreachable.
- Device connectivity must not require validated internet access, so a LAN-only network reaching a self-hosted server counts as online.
- When a self-hosted server is on the local network, the app must request local-network permission with a clear purpose string, and must surface a distinct, actionable status when that permission is denied — never conflating it with 'backend unreachable'.
- When the app is configured to reach a server over plain HTTP, a persistent warning banner must appear above all content including onboarding, derived from the same policy that governs whether the request layer permits the connection, and enabling that mode must require an explicit risk acknowledgement.
- Each active, compiled-in driver module that ships a brand mark must display it untinted after the status icons, in a horizontally scrollable strip that can never push the status icons off a narrow screen; a module without a mark stays active and shows nothing. At most one pump brand mark can ever appear.
- The glucose hero must show the current value at hero scale with its trend glyph in the same colour, the unit label, a relative-age caption coloured by freshness, and the secondary metrics IoB, basal rate (with a Sleep / Exercise / Automated suffix), battery percentage and reservoir units when present. With no reading it must show '--' and the unit label only, with no age caption, badge or secondary metrics.
- When the glucose reading is too stale, the hero value and trend glyph must lose their severity colour and render de-emphasised grey, gain a 'Too old' badge, and the card's spoken description must announce that the reading is stale and its age BEFORE reading out any other metric.
- Glucose severity colour must be a function of the stored mg/dL value alone with no unit input, so the display-unit choice can never change the severity a user sees. The colour must encode severity, not direction: low and high share one colour, urgent-low and urgent-high share another. Boundary behaviour must match Android exactly (55 urgent-low, 56 low, 70 low, 71 in-range, 179 in-range, 180 high, 249 high, 250 urgent-high).
- All storage, transport, threshold comparison, alerting, chart geometry, Time-in-Range bucketing, CV% and GMI must operate on canonical mg/dL integers. Only an enumerable set of rendered strings may convert: hero value and unit label, chart Y-axis labels, chart tooltip, TIR bucket-boundary and target labels, CGM-stats mean and standard deviation, and the bolus table's BG column.
- mg/dL to mmol/L conversion must use exactly one constant, 18.0156, shared with the backend and the Apple Watch app, converting once from the most precise mg/dL source and rounding last to one decimal. No second constant may exist anywhere in the codebase.
- The iPhone app and the Apple Watch app must produce byte-identical glucose strings for the same stored mg/dL value, pinned by a shared cross-surface fixture (54→3.0, 70→3.9, 99→5.5, 100→5.6, 120→6.7, 180→10.0, 250→13.9, 400→22.2, 20→1.1, 500→27.8).
- Glucose spreads (standard deviations and differences) must convert through a distinct, offset-free path and always render with one decimal in either unit; the value converter must never be used for a spread.
- Every number the dashboard displays must use a dot decimal separator and half-up rounding regardless of device locale, so no screen mixes separators and no percentage, mean or unit value differs by a digit from the equivalent Android string.
- Changing the glucose unit anywhere — in Settings or via a backend reconcile — must re-render every glucose surface on the phone and propagate to the Watch app and every complication and widget without an app restart. The unit must be reconciled from the backend on every dashboard load, never gated behind another setting's staleness clock.
- Data freshness must be classified into fresh / stale / too-stale using half-open boundaries (exactly at a threshold crosses into the worse tier) with per-source policies: CGM stale at 6 minutes and too-stale at 15; pump metrics (IoB, basal, battery, reservoir) stale at 15 minutes and too-stale at 60. Negative ages classify as fresh for display purposes only.
- Freshness must re-evaluate on a wall-clock timer independent of new data arriving, at one quarter of the source's stale threshold clamped to 2–30 seconds, so a staleness badge appears and the age caption grows while the app sits open and no new reading arrives.
- A staleness badge must render nothing at all when fresh, 'Stale' in amber when stale, and 'Too old' in grey when too-stale — such that a fully fresh screen contains zero staleness-badge elements as an assertable test invariant.
- On every Apple Watch complication, Smart Stack entry and Lock Screen or Home Screen widget, the fresh→stale→too-stale degrade must be pre-scheduled as timeline entries at the exact policy boundaries computed from the reading's own timestamp, so the surface degrades on time even when the extension is never woken with new data.
- The alert-floor gate must require strictly-fresh data measured against the CGM's own sensor timestamp — never a poll, receive or wall-clock time — must tolerate up to 60 seconds of future skew, and must be the single shared definition used by display, alerting and any 'this device is watching you' claim. Stale and too-stale must both suppress the alarm and flip the claim to an honest 'not watching'.
- Any 'this device is watching' claim must decay on its own schedule with no new data, must emit a pessimistic value first when its inputs are still unknown, and must be designed to under-claim rather than over-claim. Because iOS provides no guaranteed-persistent status surface, the absence of a widget, complication or Live Activity must never be presentable to the user as evidence that monitoring is active.
- The trend chart must render on a fixed 40–300 mg/dL Y axis with dashed grid lines and labels only at the low, high and urgent-high thresholds converted to the user's unit, over a translucent target-range band. Readings outside the axis must be pinned to the edge while keeping their true severity colour. Glucose must be drawn as unconnected dots, on top of every overlay.
- The chart must draw, back to front: target band, threshold grid lines, Y-axis labels, X-axis time labels, sleep/exercise mode bands, the stepped basal area occupying the bottom quarter of the plot, the IoB area and line on an independent right-hand scale, staggered diamond bolus markers with unit and type labels, then glucose dots.
- Chart time-axis label format and tick count must follow the visible span using the Android rules, and the chart must abort drawing safely on a zero-span or zero-size plot area rather than dividing by zero.
- The chart legend must list only the series actually present in the loaded data.
- From Home the user must be able to expand the chart into a detail view supporting pinch-zoom down to a 15-minute minimum span and no wider than the selected period, drag-pan bounded by the period start and 'now', double-tap to reset the viewport, and tap-to-inspect a reading via a tooltip showing the value with its unit label and the local time — accepting a tap only within 3% of the visible span (clamped to 1–5 minutes) of a reading, and dismissing the tooltip on any other gesture.
- The chart detail view must present in landscape and must restore the previous orientation posture on exit.
- Period selection must be shared between the Home chart card and the chart detail view, and between the Recent Boluses card and the Bolus History screen, so a change made in one is reflected in the other.
- The dashboard must maintain five independent period selections (chart, Time in Range, CGM stats, insulin summary, bolus list) with the Android defaults, and must preserve the distinction that Time in Range and CGM stats use rolling now-minus-N-hours windows while insulin summary and bolus lists use day-boundary-aligned windows anchored on a configurable day-boundary hour.
- Period choices must be filtered by the user's local retention setting, and a selection outside the available set must fall back to the first available period; the CGM Stats card must additionally push the corrected selection back into shared state.
- Time in Range must be computed by a database aggregate over readings between 20 and 500 mg/dL using the Android bucket comparisons, and rendered as a five-segment stacked bar in which the last non-zero segment absorbs all rounding, with a legend labelling bucket boundaries in the user's unit, a target caption in the user's unit, and a quality assessment label.
- CGM statistics must report mean, sample standard deviation using the n−1 denominator, CV%, GMI, CGM-active percentage against an expectation of 12 readings per hour, and the valid reading count, with the Android quality bands. GMI and CV% must be derived from the raw mg/dL mean and must never be unit-converted.
- Insulin summary must report total daily dose, the basal/bolus split as a stacked bar and legend, food and correction portions per day, and a per-category breakdown in a fixed display order. Basal must be computed as a segment integral with each segment capped at two hours and the final segment extended to now, against a denominator measured from now and floored at one day, so gaps under-report rather than extrapolate.
- The app must not present a bolus's requested meal and correction portions as if they summed to the delivered units; partial delivery, cancellation and automated boluses can legitimately break that identity.
- Recent Boluses must show the five most recent events in descending time order with time, units, a type badge, BG at event and IoB at event, plus a derived plain-language reason, and must offer a path to a full Bolus History screen listing all events for the period.
- BG at event and IoB at event must be cross-referenced to the nearest reading within plus or minus five minutes, showing '--' when no reading falls in that window. The BG column must render in the user's display unit with a unit label — a deliberate correction of the current Android behaviour, which prints a raw mg/dL integer to mmol/L users.
- Glucose thresholds must be cached locally with the defaults 55/70/180/250, refreshed from the backend when older than 15 minutes, each value coerced into 20–500, and the entire update rejected — leaving the previous values intact — when the ordering invariant fails. Thresholds must remain mg/dL on the wire and in storage regardless of the display unit.
- Glucose readings outside 20–500 mg/dL and boluses outside 0–25 units must be rejected at the model boundary and silently skipped when read back from local storage, never surfaced as an error row; the dashboard must fall back to the next valid reading or to the no-data state.
- Local pump and CGM data must be encrypted at rest using the platform's data protection so it is unreadable before first device unlock, and credentials must be stored in the Keychain with an accessibility class that permits background use.
- The tab bar must be capability-driven: Home, Alerts and Settings always present, and an AI Chat tab only when a backend is configured. Backend-only destinations must remain registered so restored navigation state and deep links resolve, redirecting to Home rather than blanking when no backend is configured, and the backend-configured decision must be evaluated live rather than captured when the navigation graph is built.
- The start destination must depend only on onboarding completion and never on session validity, so an expired or absent session still reaches locally stored pump and CGM data offline.
- A tappable session banner must appear for an expired session unconditionally, and for an unauthenticated state only when a backend is configured, and must never appear while authentication state is still initialising. It must not appear during onboarding.
- Detail screens must hide the tab bar and provide a back affordance.
- The app must support System, Dark and Light theme selection, applying it live without restart, using the Android palette exactly. Semantic colours (glucose severity, bolus type, meal confidence, and the amber safety-note treatment that must never be the error red) must be identical in both appearances, and the safety-note treatment must resolve against the effective theme so a forced Light or Dark selection is respected.
- Every element that carries a Compose test tag today must carry the same string as an accessibility identifier, and 'a fresh screen contains zero staleness badges' must remain an assertable condition.
- Every card must expose a single combined VoiceOver description matching the Android content-description text, with glucose values spoken in the user's unit using the spoken unit names ('milligrams per deciliter' / 'millimoles per liter').
- The hero value and other fixed-size numerals must remain legible and non-truncating across Dynamic Type sizes including the accessibility sizes.
- Markdown prose rendered anywhere in the app must strip image markdown and HTML image tags before rendering so no outbound image fetch can be triggered by generated content, and must only open http and https links.
- Every 'now' used by freshness classification, chart windows, day-boundary alignment, the basal integral and the TDD denominator must come from an injectable clock rather than a direct system-time read, so the entire time-dependent surface can be exercised deterministically — the iOS Simulator cannot have its clock changed independently of the host Mac.
- Debug builds must include a simulated pump driver, selectable in the Simulator, that can drive every connection state, replay recorded CGM / IoB / basal / bolus histories, and inject stale, out-of-range, future-dated and clock-rewound values — because Core Bluetooth does not exist in the iOS Simulator and no BLE-dependent state on this dashboard can otherwise be reached during development.
- Debug builds must retain the compressed fast-staleness policy (stale at 20 seconds, too-stale at 45 seconds) behind a toggle that is hard-gated so it can never be reached in a TestFlight or Release build.
- The Apple Watch app and the iPhone app must link the same formatting, freshness and threshold modules rather than reimplementing them, so a drift in either is a compile-time impossibility rather than a test that someone forgot to run.

### IMPOSSIBLE (3)
- A persistent, non-dismissible OS-level status surface mirroring the Android foreground-service notification ('Connected to pump' / 'Server alerts paused' / 'Floor watching' / 'Floor NOT watching') :: iOS has no ongoing or non-dismissible notification. `UNNotificationRequest` produces a user-dismissible banner; there is no `setOngoing(true)` equivalent and no persistent notification tied to app execution, because there is no foreground service to tie it to. Live Activities are the nearest construct and they are explicitly time-bounded and user-dismissible: an activity runs at most 8 hours active plus roughly 4 more hours on the Lock Screen before the system ends it, the user can swipe it away at any time, and starting one normally requires the app to be in the foreground (push-to-start exists from iOS 17.2 but still cannot guarantee persistence). Widgets and Smart Stack complications are budgeted pull surfaces the user must choose to place. Nothing on iOS can guarantee 'this status is always visible while the app is monitoring'. :: ALT: A three-part honest substitute: (1) an ActivityKit Live Activity started when a pump session begins, showing pump-link state and the alert-floor claim on the Lock Screen and Dynamic Island, refreshed via ActivityKit updates and pre-scheduled `staleDate` so it self-degrades to 'not watching' rather than going quietly wrong; (2) a Home Screen / Lock Screen widget and a watchOS complication carrying the same claim with WidgetKit timeline entries scheduled at the freshness boundaries, so the claim decays on time with zero refresh budget; (3) a one-shot, user-dismissible local notification when the floor stops watching, so the transition is announced even if no persistent surface is placed. The honesty contract must invert relative to Android: because no surface is guaranteed present, the ABSENCE of a 'watching' surface must never be read by the user as 'watching' — the in-app status row must be the authoritative claim and the onboarding copy must say so.
- Guaranteed fixed-cadence background refresh (Android's 15-second pump poll and the ~30-second BLE keep-alive) keeping the dashboard, complications and alert floor continuously current while the app is not in the foreground :: iOS provides no periodic background execution to a suspended app. `BGAppRefreshTask` is opportunistic and budgeted by the system against user launch patterns — `earliestBeginDate` is a floor, not a schedule, and intervals of minutes to hours are normal; `BGProcessingTask` requires charging and device idle. Core Bluetooth's `bluetooth-central` background mode wakes the app on peripheral connection/disconnection and on value updates for characteristics you are subscribed to — it does not provide a timer, and background scanning is restricted to explicit service UUIDs with duplicate filtering forced on. There is no supported way to run a 15-second loop, and any attempt (audio session abuse, location background mode without a real location feature) is an App Review rejection under Guideline 2.5.4 and drains the battery. :: ALT: Compose three mechanisms and design the display to be honest about the gaps. (a) Where a driver's protocol supports it, subscribe to a notifying characteristic so the PUMP drives the cadence and iOS wakes the app per notification; combine with `CBCentralManagerOptionRestoreIdentifierKey` state preservation and restoration so the app is relaunched into the background after termination and can re-establish. (b) Register a `BGAppRefreshTask` for opportunistic settings reconciliation and sync-queue drain, never for anything the user's safety depends on. (c) For backend-configured users, have the server push new readings via APNs. Then let the ported freshness policy do its job: whatever is missed shows as STALE and then TOO_STALE on schedule, the alert floor refuses to claim it is watching, and complications degrade via pre-scheduled timeline entries. The right framing for the PRD is that iOS trades guaranteed cadence for guaranteed honesty — the app must never present a gap as continuity.
- Shipping a custom watch face (the Android `watchface/` module and the phone→watch face push/install flow) :: watchOS does not support third-party watch faces. There is no API to define a face, and no mechanism for an app to install one; the Android app's WatchFaceInstaller/WatchFaceReceiveService APK-push flow has no counterpart and would in any case be dynamic code loading, which App Store Guideline 2.5.2 forbids. :: ALT: Two things, both real and both worth shipping. (1) WidgetKit complications for watchOS (`WidgetFamily.accessoryCircular` / `.accessoryCorner` / `.accessoryRectangular` / `.accessoryInline`) that the user adds to an Apple-provided face — this delivers the actual capability the custom face existed to provide (glucose, trend, IoB, age, and the graph as a rectangular complication rendered with the same shared `Canvas` code) and additionally reaches the Smart Stack and the iPhone Lock Screen with the same widget code. (2) Watch Face Sharing (watchOS 7+): bundle a curated `.watchface` file in the app that pre-configures an Apple face with the app's complications already placed, and offer an 'Add to Watch' button — the user taps once and gets a ready-made face. That is as close to 'we ship you a face' as the platform allows, and it is a genuinely good experience.

### NEEDS REDESIGN (7)
- Driver-contributed dashboard cards (runtime plugin registry + declarative CardElement rendering) :: Android discovers plugins at runtime through a ConcurrentHashMap-backed `PluginRegistry` and renders their cards from a declarative `DashboardCardDescriptor`/`CardElement` tree precisely BECAUSE the plugin code is dynamically loaded and must not touch host UI. On iOS, drivers are compile-time-linked Swift modules (App Store Guideline 2.5.2 forbids dynamic code loading), so runtime discovery, the undefined iteration order it caused, and the sandboxing rationale for a data-only UI contract all change. The user-visible outcome (cards below the core cards, ordered by ascending priority, tappable when hasDetail) must be preserved exactly. :: REC: Keep the declarative descriptor contract (option 1). It costs almost nothing to port (CardElement is a 9-case sum type with trivial preconditions), it keeps driver modules free of any UI framework so the SAME module can feed the watchOS app and future surfaces, and it preserves the priority/id/title/hasDetail contract the backend and the E2E identifiers (`plugin_card_<id>`) already encode. Keep the explicit sort of active module IDs even though a static array is deterministic — it costs nothing and keeps phone/Watch badge order identical.
- Dashboard data flows, settings reconciliation, and manual refresh under the iOS app lifecycle :: The dashboard's freshness assumptions rest on Android's foreground-service poll loop (15 s fast poll for IoB/basal/CGM, which is also a BLE keep-alive because the pump drops idle links at ~30 s). iOS has no foreground service. Once the app is backgrounded it is suspended within seconds; the only background execution available is (a) Core Bluetooth wake-ups via `UIBackgroundModes: bluetooth-central` + `CBCentralManagerOptionRestoreIdentifierKey`, which fire on connect/disconnect and on characteristic notifications for peripherals you have an outstanding connect request for — never on a timer — and (b) `BGAppRefreshTask`, which is opportunistic, budgeted by usage, and typically minutes-to-hours apart. So the 15 s cadence and the ~30 s keep-alive cannot both be guaranteed, and the dashboard must therefore treat 'the app was suspended' as a first-class state rather than assuming continuous polling. :: REC: Do all three, layered, with option 1 as the load-bearing contract. Foreground behaviour is the parity surface and must be exact (same order, same 500 ms stagger, same concurrent-refresh drop via a compare-and-swap flag, same 'CGM result also goes through the polling orchestrator so a manual refresh reaches the alert floor'). Add an explicit scenePhase-active refresh so returning to the app never shows a value the app could have refreshed but didn't. Then let each driver module opt into notify-characteristic background delivery, and let backend-configured users additionally receive push. Critically: do NOT let the display layer paper over the gap — if the data is 40 minutes old because iOS suspended the app, the hero must be grey with 'Too old', which is exactly what the ported freshness policy already does.
- Reactive settings transport across the app, its widget/complication extensions, and the Watch app :: Android uses a SharedPreferences `OnSharedPreferenceChangeListener` callbackFlow as the reactive transport for glucose unit, theme, thresholds, retention, debug toggles and category labels — one process, instant propagation. On iOS the same values are needed by at least three processes on two devices: the app, its WidgetKit extension(s) (Lock Screen / Home Screen / Smart Stack / watch complications), and the watchOS app. `UserDefaults(suiteName:)` in an App Group shares storage between the app and its extensions on the SAME device, but `UserDefaults.didChangeNotification` does not reliably fire cross-process, and App Groups do not span the phone↔Watch boundary at all. :: REC: Option 1. `updateApplicationContext` is the right WatchConnectivity primitive for settings (it coalesces, survives the counterpart being unreachable, and delivers on next wake), and explicit `WidgetCenter` reloads are the only supported way to make an extension notice a defaults change. The PRD requirement is behavioural, not mechanical: a unit or theme change made anywhere must reach every surface without an app restart, and no surface may ever display a value in a different unit from the phone.
- Freshness ticking on watchOS complications, Smart Stack, and Lock Screen widgets :: The single most safety-load-bearing behaviour in this subsystem is that the FRESH→STALE→TOO_STALE degrade happens on a wall-clock ticker WITHOUT new data arriving, so a dead sensor cannot leave a confident green number on screen. In-app that is a `TimelineView` and ports cleanly. On every widget/complication surface it cannot be: WidgetKit is a pull model with a hard reload budget (roughly 40–70 timeline reloads per day for a placed iOS widget; watchOS complications for the active face get more but are still budgeted), and a widget process cannot run a timer. :: REC: Option 1, and make it a hard PRD requirement. It is a perfect structural fit: WidgetKit's timeline model was designed for exactly this. Every complication and widget timeline must contain the two boundary entries derived from the same shared `FreshnessPolicy` the phone uses, so a complication whose data stops arriving turns grey at the 15-minute mark on its own. Also add `.privacySensitive()` where appropriate and handle `redactionReasons` for watchOS Always-On (SwiftUI throttles AOD updates to roughly once per minute and dims the UI — the age caption must remain legible and honest under redaction).
- Chart detail's forced landscape orientation :: Android sets `Activity.requestedOrientation = SCREEN_ORIENTATION_SENSOR_LANDSCAPE` on enter and restores it on dispose. iOS has no per-screen orientation property on a SwiftUI view; orientation is negotiated between the Info.plist `UISupportedInterfaceOrientations`, the root view controller's `supportedInterfaceOrientations`, and (iOS 16+) `UIWindowScene.requestGeometryUpdate(.iOS(interfaceOrientations:))` paired with `setNeedsUpdateOfSupportedInterfaceOrientations()`. There is also no 'sensor landscape' concept, and the interaction with the user's Portrait Orientation Lock is a HIG sensitivity Android does not have. :: REC: Option 1 for parity — the wide chart genuinely needs the landscape aspect ratio and the Android UX made that call deliberately. Implement it via `requestGeometryUpdate`, which does override the device rotation lock when the app's declared orientations permit it, and always restore the previous mask on dismissal (an unrestored mask is a classic iOS bug). Keep the graceful-degradation posture Android has: if the window scene is unavailable, log and skip rather than crashing. Note that this constrains the app to iPhone; on iPad the orientation request is not honoured the same way.
- Numeric locale and rounding consistency across the dashboard :: Two separate defects compound on iOS. (1) Android forces Locale.US inside GlucoseFormat but uses the DEVICE locale for IoB, basal, battery, reservoir, TIR percentages, CV%, GMI and their accessibility strings — so a comma-decimal device today mixes '6.7 mmol/L' with '1,23u'. (2) More seriously, Java's Formatter documents `%f` as HALF_UP on the decimal expansion, while C/Foundation `String(format:)` rounds the exact binary value half-to-EVEN. That is not theoretical: a TIR bucket of exactly 12.5% renders '13%' on Android and '12%' in a naive Swift port, and a mean glucose of exactly 100.5 renders '101' vs '100'. Any per-value cross-surface comparison would silently drift. :: REC: Option 1. Use `Locale(identifier: "en_US_POSIX")` and explicit half-up rounding (`Decimal` with `.plain`, or `NumberFormatter` with `roundingMode = .halfUp` — note NumberFormatter defaults to `.halfEven`) for every number the dashboard renders, and pin the exact-tie cases (12.5%, 100.5 mean, 0.125 U) in tests against the Android strings. This is a deliberate, documented improvement over Android rather than a defect port, and it is the only option that keeps the phone, the Watch, the widgets and the backend emitting identical strings.
- Cleartext / local-network access to a self-hosted backend (the surface behind the insecure-HTTP banner) :: The banner itself ports trivially, but the capability it warns about does not. iOS App Transport Security blocks cleartext HTTP by default and requires an explicit Info.plist posture; separately, iOS 14+ Local Network Privacy requires `NSLocalNetworkUsageDescription` and shows a user permission prompt before ANY connection to a local-network host, including a self-hosted backend on 192.168.x.x or a `.local` name. Android needed neither. A denied local-network permission is a silent, unrecoverable-in-app failure mode (the user must re-enable it in Settings) that the status row and banner have no state for today. Crucially, the iOS Simulator does not enforce Local Network Privacy — it uses the Mac's networking — so this path CANNOT be validated in the Simulator and will only fail on device. :: REC: Option 1 as the shipped default, with the existing 'I understand the risk' acknowledgement gate extended to also explain the iOS local-network permission, plus a new honest status: the reachability indicator must distinguish 'local network permission denied' from 'backend unreachable', with a tap target that deep-links to `UIApplication.openSettingsURLString`. Do not reach for `NSAllowsArbitraryLoads` unless field testing proves option 1 excludes real self-hosted setups — it invites an App Review conversation for a capability most users will not need. Whichever is chosen, add `NSBonjourServices` if any discovery is done, and make the Simulator's non-enforcement an explicit note in the dev-loop docs so the developer tests this path on hardware before every TestFlight upload.

### SAFETY-CRITICAL (12)
- Glucose validity bounds are 20–500 mg/dL and are enforced in FOUR independent places that must all be ported: CgmReading's constructor (MIN_MG_DL=20 / MAX_MG_DL=500, throws), DashboardComputations VALID_GLUCOSE_MIN/MAX (filters CGM stats input), the TIR SQL 'glucoseMgDl BETWEEN 20 AND 500' filter, and HomeViewModel's threshold coercion MIN_THRESHOLD=20 / MAX_THRESHOLD=500. EnrichedBolusEvent additionally requires bgAtEvent in 20..500. Room rows outside these bounds are silently DROPPED at the entity->domain mapper (returns null), so an out-of-range value never renders at all.
- Unit conversion has exactly ONE constant, GlucoseFormat.MGDL_PER_MMOL = 18.0156, mirroring the backend src/core/units.py MGDL_PER_MMOL and the web constant. The code carries an explicit instruction never to introduce a second value (18.02 / 18.0182) because drift desyncs client from server. The rule is convert ONCE from the most precise mg/dL source and round LAST (one decimal for mmol). Clinical anchors that must hold: 70->3.9, 180->10.0, 120->6.7, 100->5.6; cross-surface fixture 54->3.0, 99->5.5, 250->13.9, 400->22.2; bounds 20->1.1, 500->27.8. Locale.US is forced everywhere in GlucoseFormat so the separator is always a dot.
- Spread conversion is a DISTINCT function: convertSpread / formatSpread divide by the factor with no zero-anchor and always render one decimal. Reusing the value converter for a standard deviation is called out in the code as a bug.
- GMI is computed as 3.31 + 0.02392 * mean from the RAW mg/dL mean and must NEVER be unit-converted (a dedicated unit test pins this). CV% likewise stays a percentage of raw mg/dL.
- Staleness policy is a documented display-safety decision requiring PM/clinical sign-off: CGM STALE at 6 min, TOO_STALE at 15 min; PUMP STALE at 15 min, TOO_STALE at 60 min; half-open boundaries (exactly at a threshold crosses into the worse state). At TOO_STALE the glucose value LOSES its severity color and renders grey, gains a 'Too old' badge, and the accessibility description says 'reading is stale, last updated X' BEFORE reading out any other metric — a stale number must never be presented as a live one.
- The freshness UI re-evaluates on a ticker (staleAfterMs/4 clamped to 2–30 s) so the staleness badge appears and the age grows WITHOUT any new reading arriving. A port that only recomputes on data arrival would leave a dead sensor displaying a confident, green, hours-old number forever.
- The alert-floor freshness gate isFreshForAlertFloor requires STRICTLY FRESH plus ageMs >= -60_000 ms (ALERT_FLOOR_MAX_FUTURE_SKEW_MS), computed against the CGM's OWN SENSOR TIMESTAMP, never the poll/receive wall-clock. STALE and TOO_STALE both suppress the alarm and flip the surface to an honest 'not watching'. Backward wall-clock jumps are guarded statefully by AlertFloor's high-water mark; the documented accepted residual fails toward alarming, never toward silence.
- Insulin/dosing display: BolusEvent enforces a hard cap of MAX_BOLUS_UNITS = 25f on units, correctionUnits and mealUnits (Tandem hardware max), re-validated on EnrichedBolusEvent. Rows violating the cap are dropped rather than displayed. correctionUnits/mealUnits are the REQUESTED breakdown and may legitimately not sum to the DELIVERED units (partial delivery, occlusion, user cancel, status opcode 0x30 with no breakdown, automated boluses with mealUnits=0) — displaying them as if they summed would misrepresent delivered insulin.
- The basal integral caps each segment at 2.0 hours and extends the final segment to System.currentTimeMillis(); the TDD denominator uses now (not the newest event) and is floored at 1 day. These choices deliberately under-report rather than extrapolate across gaps — a port that changes either half desyncs numerator and denominator and misstates total daily dose.
- Glucose threshold updates from the backend are rejected wholesale when the ordering invariant fails (urgentLow > low || low >= high || high > urgentHigh), after each value is coerced into 20..500. A rejected update leaves the previous cached thresholds intact rather than applying a partially-valid range that would mis-band and mis-bucket readings.
- UNIT-HANDLING GAP IN CURRENT BEHAVIOR: the Recent-Boluses and Bolus-History 'BG' column prints the raw mg/dL integer with no conversion and no unit label. An mmol/L user reading '65' in that column is being shown mg/dL. Every other glucose surface converts. Decide explicitly whether the Swift port replicates or fixes this.
- Locale inconsistency: GlucoseFormat forces Locale.US, but the hero's IoB / basal / battery / reservoir values and their accessibility strings, the TIR percentages, and the CGM-stats CV%/GMI/active% use Kotlin's default-locale String.format. On a comma-decimal device the dashboard mixes '6.7 mmol/L' with '1,23u' for insulin on board.

### CAPABILITY NAMES (25)
  Home dashboard composition and vertical order | Status icon row (BLE / sync / backend reachability) — documented in docs/mobile/status-icons.mdx | Active-plugin brand indicators | Glucose hero (primary value + trend arrow + unit label + freshness + secondary metrics) | Glucose color banding (thresholds) | Trend arrow rendering | Glucose unit handling (mg/dL vs mmol/L) — GlucoseFormat | Freshness / staleness classifier and per-source policy | Freshness UI: ticking re-evaluation and staleness badge | Glucose trend chart — base rendering | Chart overlays: basal, activity-mode bands, IoB, bolus markers, legend | Chart interaction: period chips, zoom, pan, tooltip (detail mode only) | Chart / analytics data windows and period selection state | Time in Range card | CGM Stats card and statistics computation | Insulin summary card and computation (dosing display) | Recent boluses card and Bolus History screen | Dashboard data flows, settings reconciliation, and manual refresh | Glucose threshold sourcing and validation | Navigation structure | Detail screens | Theme system | Alert-floor freshness gate (defined in this subsystem, consumed by alerting) | Domain model validation and persistence mapping | Shared presentation utilities (insecure-HTTP indicator, markdown sanitization)


================================================================================
## Alerting and notifications  [alerting]
================================================================================

### iOS REQUIREMENTS (34)
- The system must deliver audible glucose alerts for urgent low, low, high, urgent high, and insulin-on-board conditions received from a paired self-hosted backend, using the exact server alert-type vocabulary (low_urgent, low_warning, high_warning, high_urgent, iob_warning) and tolerating unrecognized future types by delivering them at the informational tier rather than dropping them.
- The system must run with no backend configured at all and still raise on-device low and high alarms computed on the iPhone from the most recent pump CGM reading, threshold-only, with no trajectory projection, no prediction horizon, and no insulin-on-board escalation, and the alert body must state that provenance and its limits.
- The system must never raise an on-device alarm from a CGM reading whose own sensor timestamp is more than 6 minutes old, must never raise one from a reading dated more than 60 seconds in the future, and must suppress all on-device alarming while the device wall clock is running more than 60 seconds behind the highest time the app has previously observed.
- The system must never raise an on-device alarm from thresholds that neither the user nor their server has explicitly set; built-in default values must never arm the alarm.
- The system must let a user set four alert thresholds (urgent low, low, high, urgent high) on the device when no backend is configured, entering them in their displayed unit, rejecting any value outside 20-500 mg/dL and any ordering other than urgent low ≤ low < high ≤ urgent high, and persisting nothing at all when validation fails.
- The system must present thresholds as read-only with an explanation that they are edited in the web app whenever a backend is configured, and must adopt a server-side threshold change within one hour without the user opening the app, whenever the app has any opportunity to run in that hour.
- The system must silently discard a server threshold response that fails validation, leaving the last known good values in place, and must never clamp an out-of-range or misordered server value into range.
- The system must preserve device-set thresholds across sign-out, and must discard server-set thresholds on sign-out or when the user removes the server URL.
- The system must deliver an urgent low alert so that the user hears it while the phone is in a Focus mode, in Do Not Disturb, or muted, wherever the platform permits, and must state in plain language, in the app and in the coverage claim, when it cannot make that guarantee.
- The system must let a user choose a distinct alert sound per severity tier from a bundled set, import their own audio file as an alert sound, and select silence for the informational (AI / data-gap) tier only.
- The system must repeat an unacknowledged urgent alert on a bounded, finite schedule until acknowledged, and must stop repeating immediately when the user acknowledges.
- The system must display an alert while the app is in the foreground — a foreground app must never silently swallow an urgent low.
- The system must let a user acknowledge an alert directly from the notification on iPhone or on Apple Watch without opening the app, and every local silencing effect — dismissing the notification, cancelling the repeat schedule, clearing the duplicate-suppression record — must take effect regardless of whether the server is reachable or responsive.
- An acknowledgment made while offline must never be reversed by a later server delivery of the same alert, and must sync to the server automatically when connectivity returns without the user retrying.
- The system must not re-alarm the same alert type more than once per 30 minutes while it remains unacknowledged, must re-alarm exactly 30 minutes after an acknowledgment if the condition still persists, and must alarm immediately as a new episode if the value recovers and then re-crosses.
- The system must not re-alarm on the device for a condition the server already announced and the user has not yet acknowledged, using the server alert history to seed the suppression window.
- The system must tell the user at all times, on every surface it offers — in-app, Lock Screen, and Apple Watch — whether anything is currently watching for lows and highs, and must never claim coverage it cannot deliver.
- Every coverage claim must carry an explicit expiry time and must degrade to an unknown or not-watching state at that time without the app running.
- The system must give a specific, actionable reason whenever it is not watching, drawn from: notifications denied, alerts disabled, alert sound disabled, critical alerts denied, time-sensitive alerts denied, notification summary may delay alerts, thresholds not configured, thresholds not yet synced from the server, no pump connection, no recent glucose reading, or coverage expired — presenting the most actionable reason when several apply.
- The system must proactively notify the user when it has stopped watching for longer than the coverage window, even though no application code is executing at that moment.
- The system must warn the user, during onboarding and in settings, that force-quitting the app from the app switcher, or not unlocking the phone after a restart, disables all background alerting until the app is reopened, and must detect and report a suspiciously long gap since it last ran.
- The system must resume alerting automatically after a background termination when the paired pump produces a Bluetooth event, without any user action.
- The Apple Watch app must show the current alert, its age computed on the watch's own clock, and whether the iPhone is currently watching; it must visually grey an alert whose underlying data has become too stale, and must show a 'no recent status' state rather than 'all clear' when it has not heard from the iPhone within the advertised window.
- The Apple Watch must never independently conclude that coverage is healthy — it must render the iPhone's claim, decay it locally, and fail closed on any missing, aged, future-dated, or unrecognized status.
- A watch complication must reflect alert and coverage state on the watch face and must change to a not-covered appearance at the coverage expiry time without requiring the iPhone or either app to be running.
- A single fresh low must produce one wrist alert experience, not several.
- The system must never retract a wrist alert into an 'all clear' state on the basis of a reading it cannot vouch for; when the underlying reading is not trustworthy it must leave the wrist alert standing and let it age out.
- The system must show the most recent 100 server alerts, prune alerts older than 7 days, offer pull-to-refresh only when a backend is configured, and explain in backend-optional mode that on-device alarms appear as notifications rather than in the history list.
- The system must render every glucose value in the user's chosen display unit while storing and transmitting canonical mg/dL, converting exactly once with the single constant 18.0156 and rounding last.
- A caregiver data-gap alert must never display a glucose value in its title, because that value is only a last-known reading during the very blackout being reported.
- Tapping an alert notification must open the app to the Alerts screen.
- The system must, in developer builds only, let the developer inject simulated CGM readings, force the backend unreachable, and compress the staleness policy to seconds, so that every alerting path — floor arming, floor firing, cooldown, episode guard, coverage decay, and wrist mirroring — is exercisable in the iOS Simulator without a physical pump or a live server.
- The system must keep its alert database and stored credentials readable while the device is locked after first unlock, so that background alerting, acknowledgment, and duplicate suppression work on a locked phone.
- The freshness policy, threshold bounds and ordering rule, unit conversion constant, re-alarm cooldown, coverage timeout, and alert-type vocabulary must have exactly one definition, shared by the iPhone app, the Apple Watch app, and every widget and extension.

### IMPOSSIBLE (7)
- Raising the system alarm volume for a low alert and restoring it on acknowledge (boostAlarmVolume / restoreAlarmVolume over AudioManager.STREAM_ALARM, with the persisted saved-volume and retry-on-failed-restore) :: iOS exposes no public API to set the output or ringer volume. `AVAudioSession` can read `outputVolume` but cannot set it; driving the `MPVolumeView` slider programmatically is a long-standing App Review rejection; and there is no per-stream volume model at all. There is also no public API to read the ring/silent switch position, so the app cannot even detect that the boost would have been needed. :: ALT: With the Critical Alerts entitlement, `UNNotificationSound.criticalSoundNamed(_:withAudioVolume:)` (or `defaultCriticalSound(withAudioVolume:)`) plays the alert at a caller-specified volume regardless of the ringer position, the silent switch, and Do Not Disturb. That replaces boost-and-restore entirely and is strictly better: no saved-volume state, no restore step on acknowledge, no risk of leaving the user's phone at maximum volume after a process death. Recast the Settings toggle 'Boost Volume for Lows' as 'Use Critical Alerts for lows' with a volume slider, and remove the restore step from the acknowledge ordering. Without the entitlement there is no alternative at all: an alert can never be louder than the user's current setting, and the app cannot tell that the phone is muted.
- Bypassing Do Not Disturb, Focus, and the silent switch by application decision (NotificationChannel.setBypassDnd(true) on the low and high glucose channels, backed by a normal install-time permission) :: On Android this is free — declare ACCESS_NOTIFICATION_POLICY and set the flag. On iOS the equivalent is `interruptionLevel = .critical`, which requires the `com.apple.developer.usernotifications.critical-alerts` entitlement. That entitlement cannot be self-granted: it is issued only after Apple manually reviews a request form, is limited to medical, health, and public-safety apps, is bound to the App ID's provisioning profile, and can be declined. Even when granted, the user must separately allow Critical Alerts at the authorization prompt and can revoke it in Settings. :: ALT: `interruptionLevel = .timeSensitive` with the Time Sensitive Notifications capability, which any developer can enable in Xcode without Apple's approval. It breaks through Focus modes when the user permits it and bypasses the Notification Summary, but it does NOT override the silent switch, does NOT override Do Not Disturb when the app is not allowed, and does NOT raise the volume. Apply for the critical-alerts entitlement on day one, ship a build that degrades cleanly to `.timeSensitive`, and make the difference visible in the coverage copy. One trap to state explicitly: the iOS Simulator does not validate entitlements, so a locally added critical-alerts entitlement will appear to work in the Simulator and then silently fail on a real device — Simulator behavior is not evidence here.
- Custom per-alert vibration patterns (the low channel's [0, 500, 200, 500, 200, 500] waveform, and the watch's urgent waveform [0, 500, 200, 500] versus a 300 ms one-shot) :: No public API on iOS or watchOS attaches a vibration waveform to a notification. Haptics for delivered notifications are chosen entirely by the system from the interruption level and the sound. `CoreHaptics` and `WKInterfaceDevice.play(_:)` only run while the app is executing and frontmost, which is precisely the case that alerting is not. The private `AudioServicesPlaySystemSoundWithVibration` vibration-pattern route is a rejection. :: ALT: Encode severity in the SOUND rather than the haptic — a distinctive custom sound per severity — and use `.critical` for lows so the system plays its strongest alert pattern. Make the urgency temporal instead of haptic: a bounded ladder of repeated notifications (for example every 2 minutes, capped, well inside the 64 pending-request limit) cancelled the instant the user acknowledges. That also recovers the intent behind Android's `setOnlyAlertOnce(false)` for lows. On the watch, the Android `showAlert` watch-face config that gates vibration has no analog and should become a per-severity notification setting in the watch app.
- Automatically restarting alerting after a device reboot (BootCompletedReceiver restarting AlertStreamService and PumpConnectionService on ACTION_BOOT_COMPLETED) :: iOS provides no launch-on-boot mechanism to third-party apps under any entitlement, background mode, or provisioning profile. There is no analog to RECEIVE_BOOT_COMPLETED. :: ALT: Core Bluetooth state preservation and restoration will relaunch the app into the background when the paired pump produces a matching BLE event — but only after the user has unlocked the device at least once since boot, and never if the app was force-quit from the app switcher. A user-visible APNs alert will also launch the app. Neither is deterministic and neither can be relied upon as a coverage guarantee. The honest product answer is a Lock Screen widget and a scheduled dead-man's-switch notification that make the gap visible, plus explicit onboarding copy about reboot and force-quit.
- An ongoing, non-dismissible, indefinitely persistent status notification whose presence proves the monitor is alive (the pump foreground-service notification, NOTIFICATION_ID 1, mutated with the coverage claim) :: Every iOS notification is user-dismissible and one-shot; there is no ongoing flag, no foreground-service notification, and therefore no notification whose existence is evidence that a process is running. The closest primitive, a Live Activity, is time-limited by the system (roughly 8 hours active and about 12 hours visible), can normally only be started while the app is in the foreground unless ActivityKit push-to-start over APNs is implemented, and is user-dismissible. :: ALT: A three-part composite: a Live Activity for the rich glanceable claim while the app is healthy; a Lock Screen and Home Screen WidgetKit accessory widget whose timeline contains a pre-computed future entry that flips the claim to NOT-watching at the exact expiry instant, so it decays correctly with the app dead and at zero refresh budget; and a scheduled dead-man's-switch notification that actively interrupts the user if the app has not run within the coverage window. The PRD must state plainly that iOS cannot guarantee a permanently visible 'we are watching' indicator — the Live Activity will lapse overnight and must be restarted on next foreground.
- Holding a long-lived server connection (the SSE alert stream) open while the app is suspended or terminated :: A background `URLSessionConfiguration` supports only discrete upload and download tasks; `URLSessionDataTask` and streaming responses are explicitly unsupported on background sessions, and a suspended app has no runloop on which to receive events. There is no background mode that grants a third-party app persistent network execution. This is the mechanism, not merely the timing: it is why AlertStreamService as designed cannot exist on iOS. :: ALT: APNs is the only path by which a server-originated alert reaches a suspended or terminated iOS app. The backend already models this — `DeviceRegistrationRequest.platform` includes an `ios` value — and today's `device_token` is only a locally generated UUID, so the wire slot exists but the sender does not. Retain the SSE client for low-latency delivery and for its liveness signal while the process is alive (foreground, or backgrounded and kept alive by Core Bluetooth), and let the existing degraded predicate arm the on-device floor for every other moment.
- A custom watch face carrying glucose and alert state, and a watch-face configuration flag gating alert vibration (the `watchface` Wear OS module and its `showAlert` config) :: watchOS does not permit third-party watch faces. There is no API, no entitlement, and no review path — Apple has never opened watch faces to developers. The Wear OS `watchface` module (digitalFull and analogMechanical variants) has no analog whatsoever, and neither does a watch-face-level configuration that the alert code reads. :: ALT: WidgetKit accessory complications that the user places on Apple's built-in faces (accessoryCircular, accessoryCorner, accessoryInline, accessoryRectangular), a Smart Stack widget, and Live Activities surfaced in the watchOS Smart Stack. The `showAlert` gate becomes an ordinary per-severity setting inside the watch app rather than a watch-face property. Expect the visual identity of the Wear OS faces to be unshippable and plan for complication-only branding.

### NEEDS REDESIGN (10)
- SSE alert stream service (AlertStreamService) — a foreground service holding a long-lived server connection :: iOS has no foreground services. A background `URLSessionConfiguration` supports only discrete upload/download tasks and explicitly not `URLSessionDataTask` or streaming responses, and a suspended app has no runloop to receive events on. The stream can therefore only exist while the app process is actually running — foreground, or backgrounded but kept alive by Core Bluetooth activity. `foregroundServiceType="dataSync"`, START_STICKY, and the persistent notification have no analog whatsoever. :: REC: Ship option 1 + option 3 immediately (they need no backend change and preserve today's behavior whenever the app is alive), and treat option 2 as the delivery guarantee to add. All the hard parts of AlertStreamService port verbatim and should be kept: the connection-generation guard, the single-flight reconnect CAS, `min(1000 * 2^min(attempt,6), 60_000)` backoff, the 401/403 jump to `max(5, attempt)`, `STABLE_CONNECTION_MS = 10_000` before resetting backoff, the idempotent start, and reconcile-pending-acks on stream open. Keep readTimeout at 75 s (2.5 heartbeats) — its purpose, bounding how long a silently dead stream can look CONNECTED, is unchanged. Explicitly reject the two folk workarounds: a background-location or silent-audio keep-alive is an App Review 2.5.4 rejection ('background services only for their intended purposes'), and PushKit VoIP pushes have been unusable by non-VoIP apps since iOS 13 (the app is terminated if it does not report a call).
- Notification channels, sounds, and versioning (three DND-bypassing channels, versioned ids, user-picked ringtone URIs) :: iOS has no notification channels, so the entire versioned-channel mechanism — and the `AlertSoundStore` channel-version keys, the `recreateChannel` dance, the legacy-channel deletion, and the documented non-atomic `incrementChannelVersion` bug — has no analog and simply disappears: sound and interruption level are per-notification-request properties, changeable at any time. What does not port is the sound source: iOS has no `RingtoneManager`, no system ringtone picker, and no way for a third-party app to read the system tone library or to play an arbitrary content URI as a notification sound. Custom sounds must be ≤30 s CAF/AIFF/WAV files resolvable by filename from the app bundle or the app container's `Library/Sounds`. :: REC: Option 2. Channel routing becomes per-request tiering with the SAME shared low/high type sets so a drifted `low_*` string is still the single point of failure to guard: low types → `interruptionLevel = .critical` when the entitlement is granted (falling back to `.timeSensitive`) with the low alert sound; high types → the same tier minus the low-specific repeat escalation; `iob_warning`, `no_data`, and every unknown type → `.active` with the default sound, which is the honest equivalent of routing to the IMPORTANCE_DEFAULT AI channel. Keep the Android rule that the informational category is the only one offering Silent, and keep hiding that picker row in backend-optional mode. Two behaviors must be stated in the PRD because they are not obvious: custom notification sounds are NOT applied to the notification when it is mirrored onto Apple Watch (watchOS plays its own default alert/haptic), so the sound choice is an iPhone-only feature; and a sound file longer than 30 s is silently replaced by the system default.
- On-device alert floor — arming gates and firing (AlertFloor evaluation on every poll) :: Every decision in AlertFloor ports verbatim — the four gates, the classification order with urgent bands first, the inclusive boundaries, the 30-minute cooldown, the ack-gated episode guard against server history with its fail-toward-alerting exception handling and its `min(serverTs, now)` clamp, the local-floor entity shape and message copy, and the never-persist rule. What does not port is the execution model. On Android the floor is evaluated by a foreground service polling every 15 s forever. On iOS there is no such loop: the app must be resumed by the system to evaluate at all, and a suspended app cannot post a notification. The only reliable resume source for this app is Core Bluetooth (`bluetooth-central` background mode with `CBCentralManagerOptionRestoreIdentifierKey` state restoration), which wakes the app on connect, disconnect, and characteristic-notification events. Compounding this, the pump drops idle BLE links at ~30 s and iOS will not run a 15 s timer while suspended, so the poll cadence itself is not under the app's control in the background. :: REC: Options 1 and 2 together, and treat option 2 as the load-bearing part. Design the background link so that each pump behavior produces a wake: an idle disconnect at ~30 s is itself a `didDisconnectPeripheral` background wake, so a connect → poll → idle-disconnect → immediate re-`connect()` cycle becomes the de facto background cadence, with a pending `connect()` surviving suspension and even app relaunch under state restoration. That yields an effective evaluation interval well inside the 6-minute FRESH window while never requiring a background timer. Also persist the per-type cooldown and acked-type sets to the App Group so a background relaunch does not re-alarm an already-acknowledged low. Keep gate ordering exactly as Android has it — in particular, keep checking notification capability BEFORE stamping the cooldown.
- Backgrounded coverage claim in the pump foreground notification (the only surface that speaks while the app is closed) :: iOS has no ongoing notification and no foreground service, so there is no notification whose mere presence proves a process is alive and whose text can be mutated from a background stream. Every iOS notification is user-dismissible and one-shot. The Android design leans on this surface precisely because it is the honest-coverage claim when the app is closed — the case iOS handles worst. :: REC: All three of options 1–3, because each covers a different failure mode: the Live Activity is the rich always-glanceable surface while the app is healthy, the widget is the surface that survives app death and decays honestly, and the notification is the only thing that can actively interrupt the user when nothing is running. State plainly in the PRD that a Live Activity runs at most about 8 hours active (roughly 12 hours visible after it ends) and that `Activity.request()` must be called from the foreground unless ActivityKit push-to-start over APNs is implemented — so it will lapse overnight and must be restarted on next foreground. Port the exact status→text mapping from PumpConnectionService (ServerActive / FloorWatching backend / FloorWatching BLE-only / NOT watching), keep the not-watching text reason-free on this surface as Android does, keep seeding from the pessimistic `current()` so a cold start into an existing outage never renders all-is-well copy, and keep the rule that a status-surface exception can never take down the BLE link.
- AlertFloorStatusProvider — the single observation pipeline shared by all coverage surfaces :: The combine graph, the `staleAfterMs/4` tick clamped to [2 s, 30 s], the reactive threshold-configured input, `retryWhen` emitting a pessimistic snapshot rather than freezing, and the pessimistic `current()` seed all port cleanly to Combine or AsyncAlgorithms. The problem is its host: on Android it lives in a foreground service that never stops, so the claim provably decays. On iOS the pipeline only runs while the process runs, which means the very mechanism that makes the claim honest — recomputation on a ticker as reading age grows — stops exactly when the claim is most likely to be wrong. :: REC: Option 2 built on top of option 1, and explicitly reject option 3 — the whole point of the Android design is that the in-app banner, the background surface, and the wrist can never disagree, and forking is the fastest way to reintroduce the lethal lie. Add `validUntil` as a first-class field on `AlertFloorStatus` so all four expiry mechanisms derive from one number, keep exactly one selector function, and keep the pessimistic `current()` seed (no CGM age, backendConfigured false) as the synchronous first frame.
- Notification permission handling and the floor's dependency on it :: `NotificationManagerCompat.areNotificationsEnabled()` collapses to one boolean on Android. iOS has no single equivalent and, more importantly, has substantially more ways for a granted permission to still produce an inaudible alert — which directly threatens the honest-coverage invariant. `UNNotificationSettings` exposes `authorizationStatus`, `alertSetting`, `soundSetting`, `lockScreenSetting`, `criticalAlertSetting`, `timeSensitiveSetting`, and `scheduledDeliverySetting` (Notification Summary, which can batch a non-time-sensitive alert for hours). None of these is a single yes/no, and two decisive inputs — the ring/silent switch position and the current ringer volume — are not readable by any public API at all. :: REC: Option 2. Request `[.alert, .sound, .badge, .criticalAlert, .providesAppNotificationSettings]` at onboarding on BOTH the server and backend-optional paths, exactly as Android requests POST_NOTIFICATIONS on both, and never request `.provisional` (provisional authorization delivers quietly to Notification Center and would silently disable every alarm). Re-read `getNotificationSettings()` on every foreground, cache it, and feed it into the same status selector. `Settings.ACTION_APP_NOTIFICATION_SETTINGS` maps to `UIApplication.openSettingsURLString`. State the irreducible residual in the PRD: without the Critical Alerts entitlement, the app cannot detect a muted phone and therefore cannot honestly promise the alarm will be audible — that limit must be written into the copy, not hidden.
- Watch alert relay (phone → wrist alert push) including setLocalOnly double-buzz avoidance :: Two separate problems. First, the transport: Google's DataClient with `setUrgent()` has no iOS equivalent with the same guarantees — `WCSession.sendMessage` requires both apps running and reachable, `updateApplicationContext` is latest-value-wins and drops payloads identical to the last (the Android design already defeats DataLayer dedup with a `_ts` key, which ports), and `transferUserInfo` queues reliably but is budgeted and gives no promptness guarantee. Second, and worse: `setLocalOnly` has NO iOS analog. iPhone notifications mirror automatically to a worn Apple Watch whenever the iPhone is locked, and there is no per-notification opt-out. The precise mechanism Android uses to guarantee 'a single fresh low is one wrist experience, not three' therefore does not exist. :: REC: Option 2. It preserves the actual Android goal (one buzz per fresh low) using the platform's own mechanism, and it makes the floor-vs-server distinction irrelevant on the wrist rather than requiring a flag that does not exist. Keep the relay as a STATE channel: the 30-minute rebuzz window and the 5-minute silent refresh become state-push cadences that keep the watch's copy inside the 6-minute CGM stale band, not buzz decisions. Port the wrist-honesty invariants verbatim, because they are platform-independent and are the safety core of this capability: a non-alertable reading skips the WHOLE relay block (never a clear, because retracting a possibly-still-true low into 'All clear' off untrustworthy data is the worse lie), and clear is keyed on the classification being in range and never on a mapping-gap nil. Accept and document the honest consequence: when the iPhone is unlocked and in the user's hand, the watch will not buzz — which is the behavior every iOS user already expects from every app.
- Wrist coverage mirror (monitoring status heartbeat) and the watch alert/complication surfaces :: The 3-minute status heartbeat is roughly 480 phone→watch transfers per day. No WatchConnectivity background delivery budget sustains that; the watch app would simply stop being woken, freezing exactly the surface whose purpose is to never freeze. Separately, the Android complication ports to a different technology: ClockKit complications are gone, replaced by WidgetKit accessory families (`accessoryCircular`, `accessoryCorner`, `accessoryInline`, `accessoryRectangular`) whose timelines are refresh-budget limited — a 15 s re-evaluation ticker is impossible for the complication (it is fine for the watch app's Alerts screen while it is frontmost). :: REC: Option 1. The watch-side half of this design is already exactly right for iOS and ports verbatim: the watch never re-derives coverage, it renders the phone's decision, stamps `receivedAtMs` on its own clock, decays after the advertised timeout, clamps that timeout into [10 s, 30 min], rejects unknown state strings, fails closed to NoRecentStatus on missing/aged/unknown/future-skewed status, and deliberately does not persist status across a watch process restart. Keep every one of those rules. For the complication, replace the 15 s ticker with pre-computed timeline entries — one entry now ('covered'), one entry at `validUntil` ('not covered') — which decays with zero refresh budget and no running code and is a strictly better fit than the Android design. Port the complication tint rules exactly, especially the rule that a stale alert may only render grey while something is watching (otherwise a dead phone behind a stale alert looks like a healthy all-clear). Port the watch Alerts screen states verbatim, including 'Not watching' with per-reason copy, 'Caregiver & AI alerts are paused — they need the backend.', 'No recent data', the TOO_STALE grey-out with 'as of Xm ago — data stale', and the amber 'Not watching — check your phone' overlay line.
- Watch-initiated dismiss → phone acknowledge :: `WearableListenerService` has no iOS analog: a watchOS app cannot wake a suspended iPhone app on demand. `sendMessage` only works while the counterpart is running and reachable; `transferUserInfo` queues but delivers whenever the iPhone app next runs, which could be minutes. :: REC: Option 3 as the primary path, with options 1 and 2 as the fallback for dismissing from the watch app's own Alerts screen. Option 3 is strictly better than what Android has and fixes a documented Android gap: the mirrored action carries the specific alert's identifier, so a wrist dismiss targets THAT alert rather than 'the newest unacknowledged server alert', and it can silence a device-computed floor alarm — which the Android wrist dismiss provably cannot, because floor alerts have no database row. Keep the ordering invariant: mark locally first, so the wrist's 'cleared' is truthful even when the backend is unreachable and the server sync defers to the reconcile pass.
- Alerting lifecycle across app and device restarts (BootCompletedReceiver, service start on login/settings, self-stop on no session) :: iOS has no launch-on-boot for third-party apps under any entitlement, so `BootCompletedReceiver` has no analog. There are no services to start or stop, so 'start on login / start from settings init / stop on logout / self-stop when no session' collapses into ordinary object lifecycle. What replaces boot-start is far weaker and needs to be designed for explicitly. :: REC: Options 1 and 2, and be blunt in the product about their limits rather than papering over them. Three facts must be surfaced in onboarding, in Settings, and in the coverage copy: nothing runs after a reboot until the user unlocks the phone at least once; if the user force-quits the app from the app switcher, ALL background relaunch — including Bluetooth state restoration and silent push — is disabled until they manually reopen it; and Low Power Mode disables background app refresh (it does not sever an established BLE link, but it removes the backfill path). Force-quit is the single most common way an iOS diabetes app goes silent, and the app should be able to detect a suspiciously long gap since its last run and say so on next launch.

### SAFETY-CRITICAL (19)
- NEVER FIRE ON A NON-FRESH READING (AlertFloor). The floor may alarm only when isFreshForAlertFloor(age, activePolicy) is true — strictly FRESH (< 6 min for CGM, < 20 s under the debug policy); STALE and TOO_STALE both suppress. Age is computed against the CGM's OWN sensor timestamp, never poll wall-clock, because a warmup/signal-loss poll can succeed every 15 s while returning the same stale sensor value.
- Wall-clock rewind guard: a BACKWARD clock jump shrinks a stale reading's apparent age and would resurrect it. AlertFloor keeps a monotonic high-water mark of every observed wall-clock time; while now + 60_000 ms < highWater, evaluation is suppressed AND the surface reports NOT watching. Residual (accepted, documented): a rewind while the process is not running leaves no mark and can under-age a stale reading — fails toward alarming, never toward silence.
- Forward clock-skew tolerance: ALERT_FLOOR_MAX_FUTURE_SKEW_MS = 60_000. Readings future-dated by up to 60 s still alarm (pump clocks drift); beyond that they are rejected. A hard age >= 0 gate would silently disable the floor for any user with a slightly fast pump clock — the same lethal failure inverted.
- Never alarm off unconfigured thresholds: AlertThresholdStore.isConfigured() (source != NONE) gates the floor. The defaults 55/70/180/250 exist only for the watch relay's classifier and were chosen by no user.
- Glucose-validity bound 20..500 mg/dL is enforced on every threshold write (updateAll/updateLocal require(), the Settings editor, and the backend-sync validator) and on the watch's received alert bg value. Out-of-range input persists NOTHING and throws / surfaces an inline error — never clamps.
- Threshold ordering invariant urgentLow <= lowWarning < highWarning <= urgentHigh (store + editor). The backend sync validates ordering on the RAW FLOATS with all-strict comparisons before rounding, because rounding two server-legal values <1 mg/dL apart could collapse them and permanently disarm the floor. An invalid server response is DROPPED, never clamped, leaving last-known-good values.
- Unit conversion: MGDL_PER_MMOL = 18.0156 is the single canonical constant (mirrors backend src/core/units.py and the web). Storage, alert detection, thresholds, and all wire values stay canonical mg/dL; mmol/L is display-only, converted ONCE and rounded LAST. The Settings editor parses mmol input back to mg/dL via *18.0156 roundToInt, and quotes mmol bounds that themselves round back inside 20..500. A drift here desyncs the client from the server (docs/contract/safety-constants.md, enforced by SafetyConstantDriftGuardTest).
- Channel routing by alert type: a drifted low_* string would route a life-threatening low off the IMPORTANCE_HIGH, bypassDnd, USAGE_ALARM channel onto the IMPORTANCE_DEFAULT AI channel — a silent safety downgrade. LOW/HIGH_ALERT_TYPES live in one shared object for exactly this reason.
- Alert delivery guarantee on acknowledge (GLY-130): the local Room mark runs FIRST and every local silence op (cancel notification, restore alarm volume, clear dedup id) runs BEFORE and INDEPENDENTLY of the server POST. An unreachable or hung backend can never leave the alarm sounding. Order is pinned by coVerifyOrder tests.
- Ack clobber guard: AlertDao.insertPreservingLocalAck never downgrades a locally-acknowledged row from a stale server echo; AlertStreamService branches on the RETURNED entity's acknowledged, so an SSE re-delivery cannot re-alarm an alert the user already acknowledged offline.
- Bounded snooze, never permanent silence: an acked, never-recovering low re-alarms exactly FLOOR_COOLDOWN_MS (30 min) after the ack, and a recovery + re-cross alarms immediately as a distinct episode. Deliberate divergence from the server (which would re-alert every evaluation cycle) to avoid alarm-spam that trains users to disable notifications.
- Episode-guard failure mode is fail-open toward alerting: a DB exception in getLatestUnacknowledgedTimestampForType is caught and treated as "no recent server alert", so a broken lookup may cost one duplicate alarm, never a missed one. The server-alert seed is clamped to min(serverTs, now) so clock skew cannot stretch the 30-minute silence window.
- Honest coverage claim (the "lethal lie"): the UI must never say "watching" when the floor cannot fire. alertFloorStatus mirrors every firing gate plus pumpConnected, the pipeline recomputes on a ticker so the claim DECAYS to NOT watching on its own, retryWhen emits a pessimistic snapshot rather than freezing, and current() seeds pessimistically (no CGM age, backendConfigured=false).
- Staleness detection is one definition in one place: isReadingAlertable is shared by the floor and the watch relay; a second staleness definition on either path is the exact silent-floor bug the design exists to prevent (guarded by AlertRelayFloorDivergenceTest and AlertFloorArmingConsistencyTest).
- Wrist honesty: a not-alertable reading skips the WHOLE relay block — never a clearAlert, because retracting a possibly-still-true low into "All clear" off untrustworthy data is the opposite lie. clearAlert is keyed on the classification being in range, never on a mapping-gap null. The watch fails closed on missing/aged/unknown status and on a backward watch-clock jump.
- no_data (caregiver data-gap) alerts must never render their currentValue in the title — it is a LAST-KNOWN reading and would fake a live glucose during exactly the blackout being reported.
- Notification permission is a coverage precondition: a floor that cannot post is not watching. It is checked before the cooldown is stamped, is the highest-priority NOT-watching reason, and is requested during onboarding on both the server and BLE-only paths.
- Logout/BLE-only transitions disarm BACKEND thresholds (so the floor can never fire off another account's levels) while preserving LOCAL device-set thresholds (so a user is not silently disarmed with nothing to restore from).
- Floor notifications are local-only on the wrist (setLocalOnly) while SERVER notifications must keep bridging — server alerts fire off a different (cloud) glucose source and are the only wrist path left while the pump CGM is stale.

### CAPABILITY NAMES (31)
  Alert type + severity vocabulary (server wire contract) | Alert threshold storage (AlertThresholdStore) | Backend threshold sync (server is master) | Local threshold editor (BLE-only mode, GLY-145) | SSE alert stream service (AlertStreamService) | Alert stream state machine (AlertStreamStateHolder) | Server alert ingestion and persistence | Notification channels, sounds, and versioning | Alert notification rendering and actions | Notification slot (stable ID) and per-server-ID dedup | Alarm volume boost and restore | Acknowledge ("Got It") flow — AlertActionReceiver | Acknowledge sync, terminal-vs-transient classification, and reconcile | On-device alert floor — arming gates and firing (AlertFloor, GLY-115) | Alert floor — shared data-trust bound (isReadingAlertable) | Alert floor — wall-clock rewind guard | Alert floor — acknowledge / bounded-snooze episode semantics | Freshness policy and the alert-floor freshness gate | Alerting-degraded predicate and alert-floor status (honest coverage claim) | AlertFloorStatusProvider — the single observation pipeline | Degraded banner copy (two modes, GLY-145) | Backgrounded coverage claim in the pump foreground notification | Alerts screen (list, empty states, refresh, per-row ack) | AlertsViewModel — state, BLE-only gating, and honest error copy | Caregiver alerting | Watch alert relay (phone → wrist, GLY-116) | Wrist coverage mirror (monitoring status) and watch alert surfaces | Watch-initiated dismiss → phone acknowledge | Notification permission handling and the floor's dependency on it | Debug fault injection for alerting | Alerting lifecycle across app/device restarts
