- Medtronic advertising payload parity (manufacturer data 0x01F9, TX-power inclusion, advertise-interval mode) :: `CBPeripheralManager.startAdvertising(_:)` accepts exactly two keys: `CBAdvertisementDataLocalNameKey` and `CBAdvertisementDataServiceUUIDsKey`. Manufacturer-specific data cannot be advertised from iOS at all. The Android implementation deliberately carries the "Mobile NNNNNN" name as manufacturer data (0x00 + ASCII name + 0x00) under company id 0x01F9 with `setIncludeDeviceName(false)`, mirroring the proven JavaPumpConnector advertisement that the pump's matcher was reverse-engineered against. iOS also gives no control over advertising interval (the BALANCED-for-first-pair vs LOW_LATENCY-for-reconnect distinction, which exists because the paired pump scans infrequently) and no control over TX-power inclusion. :: ALT: Advertise the SAKE service UUID (0xFE82 first-pair / 0xFE81 reconnect) plus the name via `CBAdvertisementDataLocalNameKey`, which iOS emits as the standard GAP Complete Local Name AD type, and validate on real hardware whether the pump's `Mobile .{0,7}` matcher reads the GAP local name or only the manufacturer-data copy. If it reads only manufacturer data, Medtronic pairing cannot be implemented on iOS by any means available to a third-party app. This is unverifiable without a physical MiniMed pump, which neither the lead developer nor the maintainer has.
- Background BLE advertising visible to a non-Apple peripheral (Medtronic RECONNECT-mode indefinite wait) :: Apple documents that when an app advertising via CBPeripheralManager moves to the background, the local name is removed from the advertisement entirely and all service UUIDs are relocated to a special 'overflow' area that can only be discovered by another iOS device explicitly scanning for those exact UUIDs. A Medtronic pump is not an iOS device and cannot read the overflow area. Medtronic's entire reconnection model is 'advertise in RECONNECT mode indefinitely because the pump scans infrequently' — which requires the phone to be discoverable for hours while the user is not looking at their phone. That is precisely the condition iOS makes impossible. :: ALT: Foreground-only Medtronic pairing and foreground-only Medtronic reconnection: the user must open the app and keep the pairing screen on screen for the pump to find the phone. An already-established Medtronic link does survive backgrounding (the `bluetooth-peripheral` background mode keeps GATT-server reads, writes, and notifications alive), so the degradation is specifically at re-establishment after any drop. There is no workaround. This makes the documented Medtronic promise "Once paired, the pump is remembered — GlycemicGPT reconnects on its own" false on iOS, and the docs and in-app copy must say so.
- Foreground service (PumpConnectionService) as an execution construct :: iOS has no foreground services, no `startForeground`, no `START_STICKY`, no service restart semantics, and no user-visible ongoing notification that guarantees process survival. There is no object corresponding to PumpConnectionService, so its documented decision — start the service only once the connection state enters {CONNECTING, AUTHENTICATING, CONNECTED} to avoid `ForegroundServiceDidNotStartInTime` when a phone cannot advertise — describes a problem that does not exist on iOS. :: ALT: The `bluetooth-central` and `bluetooth-peripheral` UIBackgroundModes plus CBCentralManager/CBPeripheralManager state preservation and restoration (`CBCentralManagerOptionRestoreIdentifierKey`) give the actual capability the service existed to provide: an indefinite BLE link maintained in the background, with the app relaunched into the background on connect, disconnect, scan match, or characteristic notification, and `willRestoreState` called before the manager reports its state. Restoration resumes Core Bluetooth work only — it cannot resume arbitrary background processing, so any non-BLE work the service did (backend sync, polling scheduling) must be re-established from within the CB delegate callbacks or scheduled via BGTaskScheduler. Keep the state-based trigger point as the moment to start a Live Activity and register background work.
- Wake locks and battery-threshold wake-lock policy (PARTIAL_WAKE_LOCK, 20 min timeout, 15 min renewal, 2 min reconnect lock, release below 5%) :: iOS exposes no wake-lock API of any kind. `UIApplication.isIdleTimerDisabled` only prevents screen dimming in the foreground and does nothing for background CPU. There is no way to hold the AP awake, no `setReferenceCounted`, no renewal loop, and no way to trade battery for reliability. The entire policy — including the safety-relevant coupling where releasing the lock below 5% battery can silently stop 15 s keep-alive polling against a pump that drops idle links at ~30 s — has no iOS surface to express. :: ALT: Rely on Core Bluetooth background mode, which keeps a connected link alive without any app-held resource and wakes the app on incoming notifications — removing the need for a wake lock for the connection itself. What is genuinely lost is app-scheduled periodic work while backgrounded: iOS will not reliably run a 15 s timer in the background. Restructure keep-alive to be pump-driven (subscribe to a characteristic the pump notifies on) rather than phone-timer-driven wherever the protocol allows, and use BGAppRefreshTask only as an opportunistic watchdog, never as the mechanism data flow depends on. Retain `ProcessInfo.isLowPowerModeEnabled` and `UIDevice.batteryLevel` as inputs to polling cadence, dropping the wake-lock release thresholds entirely.
- Permanently visible ongoing notification carrying the alert-floor honesty text :: The Android foreground-service notification is unconditional, permanent, silent, low-importance, undismissable, and always present while the service runs — and it is the only backgrounded surface stating whether anything is watching for lows and highs, with `lastFloorStatus` caching existing specifically so a redundant service start cannot overwrite "Alerts degraded — this phone is NOT watching for lows or highs" with reassuring copy. iOS has no undismissable persistent notification. Live Activities, the closest construct, are user-dismissable, end after roughly 8 hours of active display, and are removed from the Lock Screen after roughly 12 hours; a standard UNNotification is dismissable and does not persist. :: ALT: A three-part substitute that preserves the safety property without claiming permanence: (1) a Live Activity on the Lock Screen and Dynamic Island showing pump connection state and alert-floor status while paired, restarted whenever the app runs and its `staleDate` passes; (2) an unconditional in-app status banner on Home carrying the same three floor strings, which is not time-limited; (3) a repeating local notification at `.timeSensitive` interruption level whenever the floor status is FloorNotWatching, so a degraded state cannot go silently unobserved after the Live Activity expires. Whatever is built must never state or imply that alerts are active when the floor status is unknown or degraded — port the pessimistic-seed and cache-don't-overwrite rules to the new surfaces verbatim.
- Guaranteed app relaunch after user termination (START_STICKY + RECEIVE_BOOT_COMPLETED equivalence) :: Android's service is START_STICKY and BootCompletedReceiver restarts it after reboot whenever a pump is paired. On iOS, CB state restoration does relaunch the app into the background after a device reboot once the user unlocks and a matching Bluetooth event occurs — so the reboot case is covered. What is not covered: if the user force-quits the app from the App Switcher, iOS deliberately suppresses all Core Bluetooth relaunch for that app until the user manually opens it again. No entitlement, background mode, or API changes this. :: ALT: Configure CBCentralManager and CBPeripheralManager with restore identifiers so reboot and system-eviction relaunch work, and implement `willRestoreState` to re-adopt restored peripherals and re-arm the pending connection. For force-quit there is no technical mitigation — the only honest response is disclosure: a prominent, permanent statement in Settings, in onboarding, and in the docs that swiping the app away in the App Switcher stops background pump monitoring, plus a detection heuristic (long gap since last successful background wake) that surfaces a Time Sensitive notification the next time the app does run.

### NEEDS REDESIGN (20)
- Device identity: MAC address as the pairing/reconnect/storage key :: Core Bluetooth never exposes a Bluetooth device address. The only identifier is `CBPeripheral.identifier`, a UUID that is stable per (peripheral, app installation, device) but is not the MAC, is not portable across devices or reinstalls, and can change for a non-bonded peripheral rotating its resolvable private address. Every layer keys on address today: `discoveredPumps` upsert, `selectPump`, `connect(address, code)`, `PumpCredentialStore.paired_pump_address`, `removeBond(address)`, and the two UI surfaces that print it. Verified upside: JPAKE consumes only the pairing code, so no crypto input depends on the address. :: REC: Introduce a `PumpIdentity` value type (opaque stable id + display name) that replaces `String address` through the whole stack. Persist the CB identifier as the primary key and the last advertised local name as a recovery fallback; on a failed `retrievePeripherals`, fall back to a service-filtered scan matching the stored name. Never print the raw identifier in the UI — show the pump's name and model, which is more useful than the MAC ever was and removes the cleartext-device-id exposure.
- Tandem BLE scan implementation (BleScanner callbackFlow) :: Android's ScanFilter/ScanSettings/ScanCallback triple maps onto a single `scanForPeripherals(withServices:options:)` call with a `CBCentralManagerDelegate`, but four properties change: there is no `onScanFailed(errorCode)` — failures surface as `CBManagerState` transitions or simply no results; `SCAN_MODE_LOW_LATENCY` has no analogue (iOS controls duty cycle); the local `seen` set that emits each device once and freezes RSSI is unnecessary because `CBCentralManagerScanOptionAllowDuplicatesKey: true` delivers live RSSI; and background scanning requires the explicit service UUID (which the code already has) with duplicates ignored and results coalesced. :: REC: Scan `withServices: [CBUUID(string: "FDFB")]` (Tandem PUMP_SERVICE_UUID), allowDuplicates in the foreground only, and upsert by CB identifier with a fresh RSSI and last-seen timestamp on every callback. Age entries out of the list after ~10 s without a sighting. Map `CBManagerState` (.poweredOff, .unauthorized, .unsupported, .resetting) to distinct user-visible messages — this closes the Android gap where a scan failure silently just stopped showing "Scanning…".
- Scan section UI (device list, RSSI display, empty state) :: Two inputs change: there is no MAC to render as the secondary line, and RSSI is now live rather than a one-shot snapshot, which makes both the frozen-value and never-aged-out behaviours pointless to reproduce. :: REC: Secondary line = detected pump model, trailing = live signal-strength glyph plus dBm. Sort by RSSI descending with a stable tiebreak on first-seen, and age out devices unseen for 10 s. Keep the blurb, the Scan/Stop toggle with its accessibility identifier, and the "No pumps found. Tap Scan to search." empty state. Move the Tandem-specific blurb text into the driver's PairingProfile so a future central-scan driver is not mislabelled.
- BLE runtime-permission sequence (scan and advertise paths merged) :: iOS has exactly one Bluetooth permission covering both central and peripheral roles, backed by `NSBluetoothAlwaysUsageDescription`. There is no multi-permission request contract, no per-role split, no location requirement at any OS version, and no `shouldShowRequestPermissionRationale`. The prompt is triggered implicitly by the first CBCentralManager/CBPeripheralManager operation that needs it — you cannot request it on a button tap independently of starting BLE work. Critically, iOS never re-prompts after a denial, so Android's "Scan button remains enabled and re-launches the dialog on each tap" is dead code on iOS. Conversely iOS gives what Android deliberately omitted: `UIApplication.openSettingsURLString` deep-links straight to the app's settings pane. :: REC: Lazily instantiate on the first Scan / Start Pairing tap, preceded by a short in-app rationale sheet that names why Bluetooth is needed (Android has none, but iOS's one-shot prompt makes a lost grant unrecoverable in-app). Read `CBManager.authorization` (.notDetermined/.restricted/.denied/.allowedAlways) to drive state. On `.denied` or `.restricted`, replace both Android denial cards with one card carrying an "Open Settings" button. Delete the API-31 branch, the ACCESS_FINE_LOCATION branch, and the deliberate pre-31 empty-advertise-permission-array decision — none has an iOS analogue.
- Permission-denied cards (separate scan and advertise variants) :: The two error cards exist only because Android requests two different permission sets. With one iOS permission there is one denial state, and the denial is effectively terminal in-app rather than retryable per tap. :: REC: One card: title "Bluetooth permission required", body naming both roles ("GlycemicGPT needs Bluetooth to find your pump and to let your pump find this phone"), and a primary "Open Settings" button using `UIApplication.openSettingsURLString`. Re-evaluate `CBManager.authorization` on `scenePhase == .active` so the card clears automatically when the user returns from Settings — Android's card never auto-cleared.
- Paired-status card (identity display and reactivity) :: Two independent changes. The subtitle currently renders the raw MAC, which does not exist on iOS. And `isPaired`/`pairedAddress` are non-reactive getters reading EncryptedSharedPreferences on each composition, so the card can go stale — SwiftUI would make this worse, not better, because it recomposes less predictably than Compose. :: REC: Make it reactive: `@Observable` view model publishing `pairingRecord: PairedPump?`, refreshed on pair, unpair, and driver swap. Card shows checkmark/Bluetooth symbol, "Connected"/"Paired", and pump name + model as the subtitle (no identifier). Keep the trailing Unpair button, but see the unpair entries — on iOS it must open a confirmation and then a Forget-in-Settings instruction.
- CONNECTING / AUTHENTICATING display and the absence of a cancel affordance :: On Android a hung driver leaves a spinner up but the OS GATT connect eventually errors. On iOS `centralManager.connect(peripheral, options:)` has no timeout at all by design — a pending connection stays pending indefinitely, in the foreground and the background. Shipping Android's "no cancel, no timeout, no elapsed time" UI on iOS produces a genuinely unrecoverable screen whose only escape is force-quitting the app, which additionally disables background reconnection. :: REC: Both. Keep the card's visual design and its three strings ("Connecting to pump...", "Authenticating...", "Working..."), add a Cancel that calls `cancelPeripheralConnection(_:)` and returns to the scan/idle state, and add a 45 s UI watchdog (longer than the driver's 30 s AUTH_TIMEOUT so the driver's own transition wins when it fires) that surfaces a retry. Do not add a cancel that silently leaves the pending connect alive.
- Tandem connect + authentication state machine (GATT setup sequence) :: The Android sequence — `connectGatt(TRANSPORT_LE)` → `refresh()` → `requestConnectionPriority(HIGH)` → `requestMtu(185)` → `discoverServices()` → enable AUTHORIZATION CCCD → auth → enqueue five more CCCDs → 500 ms settle → CONNECTED — contains four steps with no iOS API. Transport selection is implicit (CB is LE-only), cache refresh does not exist (see impossible), connection-priority requests do not exist (the peripheral requests parameters, not the central), and MTU is negotiated automatically at ~185 with `maximumWriteValueLength(for:)` reporting the result. CCCD writes become `setNotifyValue(true, for:)`. :: REC: Implement the driver as a Swift actor exposing `connect(identity:code:) async throws`, wrapping each CB delegate callback in a continuation so the ordering (authorization CCCD first, auth, then the remaining five subscriptions) is expressed as straight-line code. Preserve verbatim: the `code.count <= 10` JPAKE-vs-legacy-V1 branch, JPAKE CONFIRMATION when a saved derived secret exists else 5-round BOOTSTRAP, the 30 s AUTH_TIMEOUT_MS, the POST_AUTH_SETTLE_MS = 500 delay before `.connected`, the guard that only promotes to `.connected` if still `.authenticating`, immediate `.authFailed` when no code is available, and clearing saved JPAKE credentials on a round-3/round-4 failure. Assert the negotiated MTU is >= the protocol's minimum and fail loudly if not, since you can no longer request it.
- Tandem reconnect policy (two-phase indefinite backoff) :: The FAST phase (10 attempts, 1/2/4/8/16/32 s capped) then SLOW phase (passive autoConnect GATT + 120 s retries, forever) exists to work around Android's lack of a durable pending connection. iOS provides that primitive natively: `connect(peripheral)` with no timeout is a kernel-serviced pending connection that costs the app nothing, survives backgrounding, and — with state restoration — survives app termination and device reboot. Reimplementing the backoff on top of it would be strictly worse: more radio churn, more wakeups, no benefit. :: REC: Replace both phases with one permanent pending `connect(peripheral)` re-armed on every `didDisconnectPeripheral` and on `centralManagerDidUpdateState == .poweredOn`, with no attempt cap and no give-up condition. This preserves the load-bearing safety property — "the pump is a medical device; if the user hasn't explicitly unpaired, always try to reconnect" — and strengthens it. Keep `.reconnecting` as the reported state whenever a pending connect is outstanding and the app is not connected. Keep the counters for diagnostics only, as Android does with `consecutiveReconnectFailures`. Explicitly forbid any give-up-after-N logic in code review.
- Tandem bond-loss / AUTH_FAILED detection heuristics :: Every trigger is a raw GATT/HCI status byte that Core Bluetooth does not expose: 0x13 CONN_TERMINATE_PEER_USER, 0x05 INSUFFICIENT_AUTHENTICATION, 0x08 INSUFFICIENT_ENCRYPTION. iOS surfaces `CBError` cases instead — notably `.peerRemovedPairingInformation` (the peripheral forgot us — the single most useful signal, with no Android equivalent), `.encryptionTimedOut`, `.connectionTimeout`, `.peripheralDisconnected`, `.connectionFailed`, `.unknownDevice`. The counter thresholds and the counter-rule are portable; the discriminators are not. :: REC: Rebuild on CBError plus behaviour. Preserve exactly: `hadSuccessfulSession` semantics (reset only by user-initiated disconnect/unpair, deliberately surviving reconnect cycles), the never-latch-authFailed-when-hadSuccessfulSession counter-rule, MAX_RAPID_DISCONNECTS = 3 for pre-connected rapid disconnects, MAX_ENCRYPTION_FAILURES = 20 accumulation before reporting, and MAX_ZERO_RESPONSE_CONNECTIONS = 3 for connected-but-silent links. Map `.peerRemovedPairingInformation` directly to a new, specific user-facing state — it is unambiguous and deserves its own message and its own remediation flow, unlike the generic "Pairing failed".
- PairingFault taxonomy and user-facing messages :: Two of five faults change meaning on iOS and two new failure modes exist that Android cannot produce. PERIPHERAL_UNSUPPORTED currently means "no multi-advertisement support"; every iPhone since the 4S supports the peripheral role, so on iOS it can only mean the Simulator or a `.unsupported`/`.unauthorized` CBManagerState. ADVERTISE_FAILED maps to `peripheralManagerDidStartAdvertising(_:error:)`. New iOS-only faults: background advertising is invisible to a non-Apple peripheral, and the peripheral removed our pairing information. :: REC: Keep all five with their existing copy (BOUND_ELSEWHERE's single-peer message is if anything more important on iOS) and add three: `.pairingInfoRemovedByPump` → "Your pump no longer recognises this phone. Open iOS Settings → Bluetooth, tap the ⓘ next to your pump, choose Forget This Device, then pair again here."; `.backgroundAdvertisingNotVisible` → "Keep this screen open while your pump connects — iPhone can only be discovered by your pump while the app is on screen."; `.bluetoothUnavailable` carrying the CBManagerState reason. Also fix the duplicated `pairing_fault` accessibility identifier while porting — give the inline waiting-card line and the FaultCard distinct identifiers.
- Advertise job lifecycle and isAdvertising flag ownership :: Android models advertising as "collect a cold Flow; collection keeps advertising alive", with a subtle finally-block rule that only a non-cancelled job may reset the flag. Core Bluetooth models it as explicit imperative state: `startAdvertising(_:)` / `stopAdvertising()` with an authoritative `isAdvertising` property and a start callback. The whole flag-ownership hazard disappears. :: REC: Use `CBPeripheralManager.isAdvertising` as the source of truth and drive UI from it plus `peripheralManagerDidStartAdvertising(_:error:)`. Keep the driver-facing API shape (`startAdvertising()` / `stopAdvertising()` on the pairing view model) so the phase-routing rule ports unchanged. Retain the documented decision that stopping advertising does not tear down any session-scoped background work, since the session-formation coupling is what owns that. Also resolve the noted Android gap: expose Medtronic's `forceFirstPair` from the re-pair entry point rather than always advertising FIRST_PAIR.
- Medtronic advertise-and-wait connection state machine (peripheral-role lifecycle) :: The state machine itself is portable, but its earliest input is not. Android's `onCentralConnected` fires on a GATT-server connection-state callback and is what cancels the pairing-wait timer, clears the fault, stops advertising, and moves SCANNING/CONNECTING/RECONNECTING → CONNECTING. CBPeripheralManager has no did-connect callback at all: the first observable signal that a central attached is a read/write/subscribe (`peripheralManager(_:didReceiveRead:)` for the Device Info service, or `didSubscribeTo` for SAKE). Separately, the SerialWorker HandlerThread maps to a Swift actor. :: REC: Treat the first `didReceiveRead` on the vendor 0x0900 Device Info service as the connect signal (the pump reads DIS during discovery), with `didSubscribeTo` on the SAKE characteristic as the fallback and the authenticate trigger. Port the rest verbatim onto a driver actor: advertising-mode selection (RECONNECT only when paired and not forced), fault clearing on connect, stop-advertising-on-connect for single-peer, the latched `.authFailed` that must not reset on the ensuing disconnect and must not start a reconnect loop, and the disconnect-while-autoReconnect-and-paired → `.reconnecting` + re-advertise path.
- Medtronic unpair / disconnect / shutdown / activation semantics :: Three Android mechanisms have no iOS counterpart. `removeBond()` is impossible (see impossible list). `close()`'s synchronous-teardown-on-the-caller-thread trick exists because a posted teardown could be dropped as a HandlerThread stops — Swift actors and structured cancellation make this a non-issue. `onActivated`/`onDeactivated` are runtime-plugin lifecycle hooks; with compile-time-linked modules the hooks become explicit enable/disable calls from the driver registry. :: REC: Keep `disconnect()` (autoReconnect off, teardown, keep the pairing) and `unpair()` (teardown, clear credentials, then instruct the user to Forget This Device) as distinct. Replace `close()` with actor deinit plus explicit `shutdown() async`. Keep the deliberate asymmetry that deactivating a driver calls disconnect and not shutdown, so re-enabling reconnects without re-pairing. Keep `reconnectIfPaired()` as a no-op when nothing is paired.
- Pairing credential persistence (EncryptedSharedPreferences → Keychain) :: There is no EncryptedSharedPreferences on iOS, and the accessibility class is a safety decision, not a formality: the app must read pairing credentials to reconnect while the device is locked in the background, so `kSecAttrAccessibleWhenUnlocked` would silently break overnight reconnection — precisely the scenario this app exists for. The existing store also has a real defect worth fixing in the port: one shared store means pairing a second driver overwrites the first driver's record. :: REC: Keychain generic-password items with `kSecAttrAccessibleAfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly` (device-only prevents the pairing secret syncing to iCloud Keychain or restoring onto another device, where it would be useless and a liability). Namespace every key by driver id: `<driverId>.pairedPumpIdentity`, `.pairingCode`, `.pairedAt`, `.jpakeDerivedSecret`, `.jpakeServerNonce`. Preserve `clearJpakeCredentials()` removing only the two JPAKE keys and `clearPairing()` removing the whole namespace — but only that driver's namespace, fixing the cross-driver clobber. Medtronic continues to store its advertised local name in the code slot.
- Unpair semantics — reconciling the two divergent Android paths :: Android has two unpair entry points with materially different effects: the pairing screen does a full teardown (driver unpair, credential clear, OS bond removal, foreground service stop) while Settings clears credentials only, leaving a live BLE session, a running service, and a stale OS bond. This is a latent safety defect on Android and would be worse on iOS, where an orphaned pending connection persists in the OS and the bond can never be removed by the app at all. :: REC: One canonical `unpair()`: cancel the pending connection, `cancelPeripheralConnection`, stop advertising, clear that driver's Keychain namespace, end any Live Activity, reset `hadSuccessfulSession`, and then present a blocking instruction sheet telling the user to complete the removal in iOS Settings → Bluetooth → Forget This Device, with an Open Settings button. Both entry points invoke it. Add the Settings confirmation dialog copy to the pairing screen too — Android's pairing-screen Unpair acts immediately on a mis-tap, which on iOS costs a manual Settings trip to recover.
- Driver activation gate on the pairing surface :: Already-decided architectural change: iOS forbids dynamic code loading, so `PluginRegistry` with compile-time factories, `PLUGIN_API_VERSION` matching, and persisted per-capability activation becomes a compile-time-linked Swift module registry. `PLUGIN_API_VERSION` mismatch is unrepresentable (the compiler enforces it). The load-bearing runtime behaviours are the single-instance mutual exclusion and the no-default-activation rule. :: REC: A static `[any DeviceDriver.Type]` registry with an `@Observable` selector persisting the chosen driver id. Preserve exactly: no default activation on a fresh install, mutually-exclusive single-instance selection with the new driver enabled before the old one is disabled (the slot is never empty), and disabling calls the old driver's disconnect. Preserve the consequence that with no driver selected there is no pump card and therefore no route to pairing — but add an ios_requirement to make that state discoverable rather than a dead end. Fix the noted defect: selecting a different driver must not leave the previous driver's credentials in a shared store (solved by the namespacing above).
- Declared BLE permissions and platform floor (manifest → Info.plist/entitlements) :: Nothing in the Android manifest transfers by name. `BLUETOOTH_SCAN`/`BLUETOOTH_CONNECT`/`BLUETOOTH_ADVERTISE`/`ACCESS_FINE_LOCATION`/`ACCESS_COARSE_LOCATION` collapse to one usage string; foreground-service permissions and WAKE_LOCK have no counterpart; battery-optimization and boot-completed have no counterpart; `uses-feature bluetooth_le required=true` has no App-Store-style device-capability filter that matters given the fork-and-build model. And unlike Android, the plugin-manifest-merge trick that put BLUETOOTH_ADVERTISE only in the Medtronic module has no equivalent — Info.plist is per-app. :: REC: One app Info.plist declaring `NSBluetoothAlwaysUsageDescription`, `UIBackgroundModes = [bluetooth-central, bluetooth-peripheral]`, `BGTaskSchedulerPermittedIdentifiers`, and `NSSupportsLiveActivities`; entitlements for `com.apple.developer.usernotifications.time-sensitive` and the App Group + Keychain sharing group the watch extension needs. Do not attempt per-driver Info.plist gating — declaring `bluetooth-peripheral` unconditionally is harmless and the fork-and-build model has no App Review that would question it. Set the deployment floor at iOS 17 or later so Live Activities, `.timeSensitive` notifications, and Smart Stack are all available unconditionally.
- Adjacent permission surfaces on the pairing journey (notifications, background execution health) :: The notification half ports well: `UNUserNotificationCenter.requestAuthorization` is a real runtime prompt, which is better than Android's deep-link-only approach, and `getNotificationSettings` gives live status re-checkable on scenePhase. The battery half does not port: iOS has no per-app battery-optimization exemption, no `REQUEST_IGNORE_BATTERY_OPTIMIZATIONS` intent, and no Doze/Sleeping-Apps equivalent to warn about. The genuine iOS threats to continuous monitoring are different: the user force-quitting the app (which permanently disables Core Bluetooth state-restoration relaunch until the app is opened again), Low Power Mode, and Background App Refresh being off. :: REC: Build a single 'Background monitoring health' card in Settings, shown whenever a pump is paired, that checks and explains: notification authorization (with in-app request), `ProcessInfo.processInfo.isLowPowerModeEnabled`, `UIApplication.shared.backgroundRefreshStatus`, `CBManager.authorization`, and whether the app was last relaunched by state restoration versus manually. It must carry an explicit, permanent warning that force-quitting the app from the App Switcher stops background pump monitoring until the app is opened again — the single most consequential iOS-only failure mode in this subsystem, with no Android analogue and no programmatic mitigation.
- Simulator-untestable BLE surface (engineering, affects the whole subsystem) :: Core Bluetooth does not exist in the iOS Simulator — CBCentralManager reports `.unsupported` and CBPeripheralManager cannot advertise. The lead developer has no iPhone and no Apple Watch. That means literally every capability in this subsystem is unverifiable by the primary developer without an architectural affordance, and Daniel's single Tandem t:slim X2 gives no Medtronic coverage at all. :: REC: All three, in that order of investment. Define the BLE seam so that everything above `CBCentralManager`/`CBPeripheralManager` is testable: a `BluetoothTransport` protocol with a real CB implementation and a scriptable fake. Ship a debug-build simulated driver that can be scripted to produce every ConnectionState, every PairingFault, wrong-code rejection, pairing-info-removed, handshake timeout, and background-disconnect — so the pairing UX, phase routing, and reconnect policy are fully exercisable in the Simulator. Add record/replay for the Tandem JPAKE and Medtronic SAKE sequences so a hardware session becomes a permanent regression test.

### SAFETY-CRITICAL (10)
- BLE bond removal decisions (Tandem BleConnectionManager.onConnectionStateChange): status 0x05 before CONNECTED, status 0x08 with no prior successful session, and 3 consecutive zero-response connections all call removeBond() + latch AUTH_FAILED. A wrong removal forces a manual re-pair with the pump physically in pairing mode, so the app stops reading glucose/insulin until the user notices. The explicit counter-rule — never remove the bond when hadSuccessfulSession is true, because transient sleep/wake encryption renegotiation resolves itself — is load-bearing and must be ported exactly.
- Indefinite reconnection is a deliberate safety decision: after MAX_FAST_RECONNECT_ATTEMPTS = 10 (~5 min of 1–32 s exponential backoff) the Tandem driver switches to SLOW phase (every 120 s + passive autoConnect GATT) and retries forever unless the user unpairs. Stated reason: "The pump is a medical device." Any port that gives up after N attempts silently stops glucose monitoring.
- AUTH_FAILED is a latched terminal state in both drivers with only generic user copy ("Pairing failed" / "The pump rejected the pairing attempt"). Bond loss, stale encryption keys, a pump that ignores requests, and a mistyped code are indistinguishable to the user, so a patient can be left with no data and no actionable message.
- PumpConnectionService wake-lock and battery thresholds directly gate whether BLE keep-alive polling (15 s cadence; pump drops idle links at ~30 s) survives screen-off. Releasing the wake lock below 5% battery and reducing polling below 15% can silently stop data flow and therefore stop the on-phone alert floor from seeing new readings.
- Foreground-notification honesty text is the only backgrounded surface that states whether anything is watching for lows/highs. lastFloorStatus caching exists specifically so redundant service starts cannot replace "Alerts degraded — this phone is NOT watching for lows or highs" with all-is-well copy. Losing that guarantee in a port would produce a false safety claim.
- Settings unpair (SettingsViewModel.unpair) clears credentials only — it leaves a live BLE session, a running foreground service, and the OS bond intact, unlike the pairing-screen unpair. A user who "unpairs" from Settings may believe the pump is disconnected while the app keeps a session; conversely the driver may keep reconnecting with no stored credentials.
- Single-peer constraint for both vendors: pairing here requires removing the pump from the manufacturer's official app. If a user pairs GlycemicGPT while relying on the official app for alerts, they can lose the vendor's alerting. This is surfaced in the Medtronic pairing screen, the Medtronic settings note, and both docs.
- Medtronic pairing is BETA / read-only and not validated against live pumps for several paths (TODO markers on the 60 s BOUND_ELSEWHERE window, the hard-coded advertised name, and live re-confirmation of the SAKE characteristic UUID after issue #844). The driver declares no PUMP_CONTROL capability and never writes to the pump.
- Pairing credentials (address, pairing code, JPAKE derived secret and server nonce) live in one shared EncryptedSharedPreferences file (AES256_SIV keys / AES256_GCM values). clearPairing() wipes the whole file. The MAC address is displayed unmasked in both the pairing screen and Settings.
- Hidden-API reflection is used for BluetoothDevice.removeBond() (both drivers) and BluetoothGatt.refresh() (Tandem). Both fail soft with a WARN log; a silent failure of refresh() can cause notifications to be dropped from a stale GATT cache, which presents as a connected pump delivering no data.

### CAPABILITY NAMES (41)
  Pairing route + entry/exit navigation | Pairing style dispatch (plugin-driven flow selection) | ConnectionState model and producers | Paired-status card (both styles) | Central-scan screen state machine | Scan session lifecycle (ViewModel) | Tandem BLE scan implementation | Scan section UI (device list, empty state, scan button) | BLE scan runtime-permission sequence | Scan permission-denied card | Pairing-code entry UI and validation | Per-model pairing instructions (Tandem) | CONNECTING / AUTHENTICATING display | AUTH_FAILED display and retry (central-scan) | Advertise-and-wait: idle / "Before you start" state | Advertise runtime-permission sequence (deliberate pre-31 decision) | Advertise permission-denied card | Advertise-and-wait: waiting state card | Advertise-and-wait phase routing rule (documented decision) | PairingFault taxonomy and user-facing messages | Advertise job lifecycle and isAdvertising flag ownership | Foreground-service coupling to session formation (documented decision) | Unpair from the pairing screen | Unpair from Settings (divergent, weaker path) | Settings pump card (pairing status + entry points) | Tandem connect + authentication state machine | Tandem reconnect policy (two-phase, indefinite) | Tandem bond-loss / AUTH_FAILED heuristics (safety-relevant) | Tandem unpair and bond removal | Medtronic advertise-and-wait connection state machine | Medtronic timers, advertising parameters and UUIDs | Medtronic unpair / disconnect / shutdown semantics | Pairing credential persistence | PumpConnectionService: foreground service + wake-lock policy | Bluetooth adapter state watcher → auto-reconnect | Foreground-notification honesty text (alert-floor coupling) | Home-screen connection status surface | Plugin activation gate on the pairing surface | Declared BLE-related manifest permissions and platform floor | Adjacent permission surfaces reached from the pairing journey | Documented user-facing pairing behavior (shipped docs)


================================================================================
## CI security gates  [ci-security-gates]
================================================================================

### iOS REQUIREMENTS (32)
- The repository must define exactly five required status checks on `develop` — `Static Analysis Gate`, `Dependency Scan Gate`, `Workflow Lint`, `Workflow Security`, `iOS Gate` — matched by job name, none using `continue-on-error`, and every one of them must be creatable on every pull request including pull requests opened from forks.
- Every required check must either run unconditionally on every pull request or report through an always-running aggregation job that treats "nothing relevant changed" as a pass and any non-success upstream result as a failure. No required check may be paths-filtered at the workflow level.
- Every required check must complete successfully on a pull request opened from a fork, using only a read-only token and no repository or organization secret. Pull-request builds must be unsigned Simulator builds, and static-analysis results must be evaluated from a local results file rather than uploaded to a service that fork pull requests cannot write to.
- The system must run static security analysis over all shipping Swift source on every pull request, blocking the merge on any HIGH or ERROR severity finding, surfacing WARNING findings without blocking, and failing closed if the analyzer itself errors or produces no results file.
- Static analysis must report, as a non-blocking warning listing the affected files, any source file the analyzer could not process, so that a target contributing zero findings because it failed to compile is never mistaken for a clean target.
- The static-analysis rule or query set must be pinned to an exact version in the repository, so that the effective analysis policy is reproducible from the repository alone and a previously green commit cannot turn red without a reviewed change.
- The system must scan every resolved third-party dependency for known vulnerabilities on any change to the dependency graph, on a weekly schedule, and on manual dispatch, failing the gate on any known vulnerability with no severity floor.
- The repository must commit its resolved dependency graph, and CI must fail if resolving dependencies from the committed manifest produces any change to that resolved graph.
- Dependency-vulnerability suppressions must live in a single reviewed configuration file, must each carry a written reason, must prefer an expiry that forces re-evaluation, and must be reviewed quarterly. No gate may be weakened by lowering a severity threshold.
- The system must lint every GitHub Actions workflow and composite action for syntax, expression, context, and embedded-shell defects, using analysis tools installed as version-pinned, checksum-verified binaries rather than third-party actions.
- The system must fail any pull request in which a third-party action reference is not pinned to a full 40-character commit SHA, across both workflow files and composite action definitions.
- The system must fail any pull request in which a composite action references the secrets context, since such references resolve to empty at runtime.
- The system must audit workflow security posture on every pull request and fail on any finding at medium severity or above that is not individually allowlisted with a written rationale, and must independently fail any workflow that combines a fork-reachable trigger with a checkout of pull-request-supplied code.
- The system must fail any pull request that introduces an Xcode Run Script build phase, a Swift Package Manager plugin, or a remote binary dependency that is not on a reviewed, committed allowlist, because each executes arbitrary code inside a build that carries a user's Apple signing credentials.
- The system must fail any pull request that commits signing material or team-identifying build configuration — certificates, private keys, provisioning profiles, App Store Connect keys, or a development team identifier in a build settings file.
- The system must fail any pull request whose entitlements or Info.plist introduce an App Transport Security exception, leave debug entitlements enabled in a release configuration, declare a background mode the app does not require, omit or leave a placeholder in a required usage-description string, or request an entitlement that Apple grants only by individual request.
- The system must fail any pull request that stores credentials or pump authentication material in the Keychain with an accessibility class that permits synchronization off-device or that is unavailable while the device is locked, and must fail any pull request that configures the local database with a data-protection class that would prevent writes while the device is locked.
- Glucose must be representable only through a type whose stored value is canonical mg/dL, whose construction enforces the 20–500 mg/dL range, and whose only unit conversion applies the single factor 18.0156. CI must fail if that type's invariants are bypassed. Automated review must additionally flag storing or comparing an mmol/L value as mg/dL, inverted conversion, out-of-range persisted values, or any other conversion factor, while explicitly not flagging user-facing mmol/L display literals or mmol/L input converted before storage.
- The public interface of the pump driver protocol module must be snapshotted in the repository, and CI must fail on any change to it that is not accompanied by a reviewed update — mechanically enforcing that no bolus, basal, pump-setting, or other therapeutic write primitive can be added to any driver protocol.
- Every pump driver's protocol framing, byte order, event-type mapping, multi-packet reassembly, and connection state machine must be exercised in CI against recorded byte traces, with no Bluetooth radio, so that protocol correctness is verified by tests rather than by review alone.
- The app must be buildable and its full user-facing behavior exercisable end to end against a simulated pump driver, in the iOS and watchOS Simulators, with no physical pump and no Bluetooth radio.
- CI must build and unit-test the phone app, the watch app, and the watch widget extension, and must lint all Swift source, treating compiler warnings and data-race-safety violations as errors.
- CI must run UI tests on both iOS and watchOS Simulator destinations, publish the result bundle as an artifact, and must not be a required check while it is stabilizing; a failure must be re-run rather than disabled, and no automatic retry may be configured.
- Platform-independent driver, domain, and unit-conversion code must be testable without Xcode or a simulator, so that those modules cannot acquire an app-layer dependency.
- The repository must ship a build-and-sign pipeline that a user runs in their own fork, under their own Apple Developer account, producing a build in their own TestFlight, and must never require the project to hold any signing identity.
- A maintainer must be able to verify, on demand and without exposing any credential value, that their fork's build secrets resolve and authenticate, and separately that a signing identity and matching provisioning profile can be materialized into an ephemeral keychain and then destroyed.
- Any credential or signing material materialized during a build must be created with restrictive permissions, removed if the operation fails midway, never written to a log, and explicitly deleted by the calling job regardless of build outcome.
- The bundled third-party attribution and licence texts must be treated as application resources: a change to any of them must re-run the build gate, and a test must fail if a bundled licence text is missing, empty, or drifted.
- Project lead review must be required on every change to workflows, the workflow-security allowlist, the package manifest and resolved graph, the Xcode project file, build settings, entitlements, Info.plist, CI scripts, security policy, and every pump driver module.
- Every developer must be able to reproduce each CI gate locally from a single committed script that CI itself invokes, so the local and CI invocations cannot diverge; gates that cannot be reproduced without Xcode must be documented as such.
- Automated dependency updates must cover GitHub Actions pins, Swift package versions, the pinned analysis tool versions, and the pinned Xcode version, with security-sensitive and major updates routed to manual review, and the resolved dependency graph must be regenerated automatically on update pull requests by a job that has a Swift toolchain.
- The documented list of required checks and the branch-protection configuration must be verified against each other in CI, so the contributor-facing documentation cannot drift from the enforced ruleset the way the Android repository's has.

### IMPOSSIBLE (7)
- Semgrep `p/kotlin` and `p/java` rule packs as the SAST policy :: These packs match Kotlin and Java syntax and produce exactly zero findings on Swift. There is no Swift-equivalent registry pack of comparable depth; Semgrep's Swift rule corpus is a small fraction of the Kotlin+Java corpus. This is a genuine loss of the specific analysis policy, not a tool rename. :: ALT: CodeQL with the `swift` language and the `security-extended` query suite is the honest replacement — first-class Swift support, versioned and pinnable query packs (which actually improves on Android's unpinned registry rule drift), and SARIF output that the existing jq severity gate consumes. Retain Semgrep `p/secrets` unchanged: it is regex/entropy-based and language-agnostic, so the in-repo PR-blocking secret lane survives intact. Add `gitleaks` as a first-class failing step to compensate for CodeRabbit's non-blocking commit status.
- Android Lint's manifest and network-security posture coverage :: Android Lint runs in the Android Gate and is explicitly named in the security-testing matrix as the verifier for HTTPS enforcement, and in the MobSF decision as the compensating control for manifest posture (exported components, `debuggable`, `allowBackup`, `usesCleartextTraffic`, weak `network_security_config`). Apple ships nothing comparable: `xcodebuild analyze` runs the Clang static analyzer, which covers C and Objective-C, not Swift, and does not inspect `Info.plist` or entitlements at all. SwiftLint is a style and idiom linter with no security or manifest awareness. :: ALT: A purpose-built `Entitlements & Plist Guard` step, as described in needs_redesign — a small deterministic checker over `Info.plist`, `*.entitlements`, and build settings covering ATS exceptions, `get-task-allow` in release, background-mode set, required usage-description strings, and non-grantable entitlements. It is narrower than Android Lint but covers the specific posture class the Android decision relied on Lint for, with no false-positive tax.
- A project-published, project-signed application binary and its central signing-secret lane :: Two independent hard constraints. Apple offers no distribution channel that would let this project ship a signed binary to arbitrary users: the App Store is out by decision and by review posture for an unofficial pump-reading app; ad-hoc distribution is capped at 100 registered devices per device class per team per year and requires per-device UDID registration; and the Developer Enterprise Program is contractually limited to distribution within the member organization, so using it here would breach the agreement. The stated distribution model — source shipped, each user builds and signs with their own account — is the consequence, and it is also what keeps GPL-3.0-only workable. :: ALT: There is nothing to replace it with, and nothing should be. The 1Password `android-signing` item, the `ANDROID_ACTIONS_SERVICE_ACCOUNT` secret, `Secrets Plumbing Check`, and `Signing Action Smoke` all lose their project-internal purpose and should be retired rather than ported. Their descendants live in the fork: a `Build Secrets Preflight` and a `Signing Smoke` that validate the FORK OWNER's own App Store Connect credentials in the fork's own Actions environment.
- Xcode Cloud as a repo-defined, fork-inheritable build/sign pipeline :: Xcode Cloud workflows are defined and stored in App Store Connect against a specific app record and team, not in the repository. Only `ci_scripts/` hooks live in the source tree. A fork therefore inherits the scripts but not the pipeline, and cannot be handed a working Xcode Cloud configuration by cloning. Xcode Cloud also cannot sign with a third party's credentials, and its free compute allotment is per-account, so it cannot be a shared project resource. :: ALT: GitHub Actions on macOS runners, which is what the fork-and-build model already requires and what the Loop/xDrip4iOS precedent uses. The full pipeline definition ships as a workflow file in the repo, the fork owner supplies their own secrets, and the build runs entirely under their account. `ci_scripts/` can still be committed for developers who choose to wire up their own Xcode Cloud, but it cannot be the supported path.
- Any CI verification of BLE behavior against a real device or a real Bluetooth stack :: Core Bluetooth does not exist in the iOS Simulator — `CBCentralManager` never reaches `poweredOn`, so no BLE code path executes. No GitHub-hosted macOS runner has a Bluetooth radio available to a test process, and none has a Tandem pump. This is strictly worse than Android, where the emulator at least presented a (non-functional) Bluetooth stack, and it compounds the fact that the lead developer has no iPhone and validates only in Simulator. :: ALT: Three layers, none of which is hardware in CI. (1) A `PumpTransport` protocol seam so every driver's framing, parsing, endianness, event-ID mapping, reassembly, and state machine is exercised against recorded byte traces in plain `swift test` — deterministic, fast, and it moves the majority of the CodeRabbit BLE review rules into executable checks. (2) A simulated in-app driver so the full app, watch app, and alerting path can be driven end-to-end in the Simulator with no radio. (3) A written hardware-validation checklist executed by the maintainer with the physical iPhone, Apple Watch, and t:slim X2, whose completion is a documented release gate rather than a CI status. Note that the Android CI never tested BLE against hardware either, so release-gate parity is preserved; what is lost is Simulator-level smoke coverage.
- A CI gate that proves a given fork's build will actually sign, install, and run :: The determining facts live outside the repository and outside any CI system: whether the fork owner holds a paid Apple Developer Program membership (a free account cannot use TestFlight at all and gets 7-day sideload profiles instead), whether their team has each required capability enabled, and whether Apple has granted any by-request entitlement. Critical Alerts is the sharp case — `com.apple.developer.usernotifications.critical-alerts` is granted per app, per team, by Apple on request, and cannot be obtained by every fork owner. CI can never assert these on the project's behalf. :: ALT: A fail-fast preflight in the fork's own pipeline that checks what is checkable and produces an actionable message: authenticate the ASC API key, resolve the team, confirm the bundle ID and required capabilities exist, and fail with named remediation otherwise. Upstream, the entitlements guard must keep non-grantable entitlements out of the committed entitlements file entirely, so no fork's build breaks on an entitlement its owner cannot obtain — which in practice means alerting is designed around Time Sensitive notifications rather than Critical Alerts.
- CVE scanning of binary XCFramework / SPM `binaryTarget` dependencies :: OSV and the GitHub Advisory Database index source packages by ecosystem coordinate. A remote `binaryTarget` is a checksummed zip of compiled code with no resolvable package identity, so it appears in `Package.resolved` in a form no scanner can match to an advisory. The Gradle lockfile equivalent did not have this hole — every locked Maven coordinate is scannable. Vendored C or Objective-C sources compiled into a target are likewise invisible to dependency scanning. :: ALT: Policy rather than tooling: forbid remote `binaryTarget` dependencies outright via the Build Script & Plugin Guard, requiring lead review and a written justification for any exception, and require `checksum:` pinning on any that is accepted. For vendored C/ObjC crypto (the Medtronic Sake analogue), keep it in-tree as source so the static-analysis gate can at least read it, and record it in the third-party licence bundle. Accept and document the residual gap the same way the Android repo documented the built-artifact secret gap.

### NEEDS REDESIGN (20)
- Security Scan Gate — the SAST job itself (Semgrep over Kotlin/Java) :: The gate shape (single unconditional job, capture exit code, upload artifact, jq-gate on severity, fail closed) is sound and platform-neutral, but the analyzer must change: Semgrep's `p/kotlin` and `p/java` packs produce zero coverage on Swift. The requirement is "same security testing in substance," so the gate survives and the tool is replaced. :: REC: Rename the required check to `Static Analysis Gate` and run CodeQL Swift + Semgrep `p/secrets` in one job on `macos-latest`, keeping the exact existing control flow. Pin the CodeQL query pack by version (`packs: codeql/swift-queries@x.y.z`) — this closes the Android gate's known reproducibility hole where pinning the Semgrep CLI still left registry rule content free to drift. Gate on the local SARIF with jq (`security-severity >= 7.0` or `level == "error"`), and upload to code scanning only on push to `develop`/`main`, because fork PRs have no `security-events: write`. Add `-warnings-as-errors` and Swift 6 strict-concurrency checking to the build gate — a compile-time data-race check with no Android equivalent, directly relevant to CoreBluetooth delegate-queue code.
- SAST scan scope, exclusions, and their rationale :: The Android roots (`app/ wear-device/ watchface/ plugins/ tools/`) and excludes (`AesEcb.java`, `.gradle`) have no Swift counterparts. The `watchface` root disappears entirely — watchOS has no custom watch faces, so there is no module to scan. The `AesEcb.java` exclusion is already inert on Android and has no successor unless Medtronic Sake crypto is re-vendored as Swift/C. :: REC: Use CodeQL autobuild over a scheme that compiles every shipping target (phone app, watch app, watch widget extension, all driver modules, and any research spikes) so scope is derived from the build graph rather than a hand-maintained directory list — this structurally eliminates the class of bug where a new module is added and nobody updates the scan roots. Keep the Android intent that non-shipped spikes stay IN scope. Do not carry the basename-scoped exclusion pattern forward; if vendored crypto lands, suppress by full path with a written reason.
- SAST pass/fail threshold, fail-closed, and unparseable-file surfacing :: The two-part policy is correct and must be preserved, but its inputs change format. Semgrep's `SEMGREP_EXIT >= 2` scanner-error check and `.errors[]` PartialParsing surfacing map onto CodeQL's exit codes and its extraction-diagnostics (files the Swift extractor failed to compile are reported in the database diagnostics, not as analysis failures — the same silent-zero-findings hazard). :: REC: Keep the hand-rolled two-part gate. Block on HIGH/ERROR-equivalent only; print WARNING as non-blocking; ignore INFO. Preserve fail-closed with a defaulted-to-fail unset variable (`${EXIT:-2}`) and preserve the behavior that a missing or empty SARIF file makes jq error the step out. Preserve the non-blocking `::warning::` for files the extractor could not process, including the file list — on Swift this is a real recurring case (macro expansion, mixed ObjC bridging headers) and losing that signal means a whole target can silently contribute zero findings while the gate stays green.
- Dependency Scan Gate — OSV-Scanner over the resolved dependency graph :: OSV-Scanner itself ports: v2 parses SwiftPM `Package.resolved` and CocoaPods `Podfile.lock`, so the three-job structure, the weekly cron, the paths filter, and the aggregation gate all survive. What changes is the shape of what it reads and, honestly, the depth of the advisory data: the OSV/GitHub Advisory coverage for the SwiftPM ecosystem is far thinner than for Maven, so a green gate carries less assurance than it did on Android. :: REC: Keep OSV-Scanner v2 pinned, `--recursive --no-ignore --config=osv-scanner.toml .`, and update the `deps` paths filter to `Package.swift`, `Package.resolved`, `**/Podfile.lock`, `*.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj` (which encodes SPM package references), `osv-scanner.toml`, and the workflow file itself. State plainly in the docs that SwiftPM advisory coverage is immature and that dependency count discipline — preferring the Swift standard library, Apple frameworks, and a small audited dependency set — is the primary control, with OSV as the secondary.
- Gradle dependency locking and the `resolveAndLockAll` completeness task :: This entire mechanism exists because OSV cannot read `build.gradle.kts`. SPM has native, always-on resolution pinning: `Package.resolved` records exact versions and revisions and is committed by default. There is no `lockAllConfigurations()`, no per-configuration exclusion set, no `resolveAndLockAll` task, and no AGP-internal-classpath problem to work around. :: REC: Do both. Commit `Package.resolved`, add a `Resolution Drift` step to the iOS Gate that fails on any diff after resolve, and build with automatic package resolution disabled so CI can never quietly float a dependency past the pinned graph. Also require that every SPM dependency is declared with `.upToNextMinor` or `.exact` (not `branch:` or unbounded `.upToNextMajor`) for crypto and BLE-adjacent packages, mirroring the Android crypto/cipher manual-review tier. This is strictly simpler than the Gradle machinery — do not port the complexity.
- osv-scanner.toml suppression policy :: The file, the syntax, and the policy port unchanged; only the triage baseline is different (an empty Swift graph, not ~1,175 Maven packages). The Android-specific escape-hatch example — a CVE fixable only by an AGP bump — has no Swift analogue; the equivalent pressure is a CVE in a transitive dependency of a pinned Apple or vendor package that can only move with an Xcode/toolchain bump. :: REC: Keep it, with zero `[[IgnoredVulns]]` entries and the same invariants: prefer bumping over suppressing, every entry carries a written `reason`, prefer an `ignoreUntil` that forces re-evaluation, review quarterly. Re-run the first-scan triage on the initial Swift graph and record the package count and result in the file header exactly as Android did, so the baseline is auditable.
- Android Gate → iOS Gate (build, unit test, lint) :: Same aggregation shape, entirely different toolchain, and it must move to a macOS runner. The Android matrix (`:app`, `:wear-device`, `:watchface`, three plugin modules) becomes phone app + watch app + watch widget extension + driver modules; the `watchface` module has no successor because watchOS forbids third-party watch faces. :: REC: Split it: run `swift test` on the platform-independent driver, domain, and unit-conversion modules (fast, cheap, and it forces those modules to stay free of UIKit/app imports — the executable version of the Android `no :app imports` review rule), then `xcodebuild` for the app and watch targets. Lint with SwiftLint (`--strict`) plus `swift-format lint`. Keep the exact `if: always()` `iOS Gate` aggregation job. Do NOT sign in this gate; unsigned Simulator builds keep the gate secret-free and fork-PR-safe. Pin the Xcode version explicitly (`sudo xcode-select -s`) — an unpinned runner image is an unreviewed toolchain bump.
- Android path filter (including license documents compiled into the APK) :: Straight remap, but the license-document subtlety must survive: `README.md`, `LICENSE`, `docs/THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md`, and `docs/licenses/**` are in the Android filter because they are bundled as APK assets and gated by `LicenseAssetsTest`. The iOS app bundles the same attribution texts as resources and needs the same drift gate. :: REC: Use that filter and keep an explicit `LicenseResourcesTest` equivalent so editing, emptying, or deleting a bundled licence text re-runs the attribution drift gate. GPL-3.0-only source distribution makes the attribution bundle a licence-compliance artifact, not a nicety. Docs/governance-only PRs should still skip the build.
- Instrumented Tests (emulator) + KVM setup + AVD cache state machine :: The entire Android emulator apparatus — the udev rule exposing /dev/kvm at mode 0666, `reactivecircus/android-emulator-runner`, the three-state AVD cache with the never-save-on-failure guard, the `-v1` eviction prefix — exists to make an emulator bootable on a Linux runner. macOS runners ship iOS and watchOS simulators preinstalled; `xcodebuild test` boots them. None of that machinery has, or needs, a successor. :: REC: Delete the AVD machinery. Add a `UI Tests (Simulator)` job that runs XCUITest on both iOS and watchOS Simulator destinations, with `-resultBundlePath` uploaded as an artifact (`if: ${{ !cancelled() }}`, 14-day retention) — the direct analogue of `instrumented-test-report`. Carry over three Android decisions verbatim: keep it NOT required so a flaky simulator cannot wedge unrelated PRs; keep no auto-retry (re-run the job, never disable the test); and if you cache DerivedData or SPM artifacts, keep the restore+explicit-save split with a `success()` guard so a failed run never poisons the cache. Note the coverage delta honestly: the database-migration and UI tests port, but nothing BLE runs here.
- Secrets Plumbing Check (1Password canary, workflow_dispatch-only) :: Purpose ports exactly — a dispatch-only, never-on-PR gate that proves credential plumbing resolves without touching signing material. The credential store changes: fork users will not have a 1Password service account, so the fork-facing path must use plain GitHub encrypted secrets in the user's own fork. :: REC: Ship a single fork-facing `Build Secrets Preflight` (workflow_dispatch only, `contents: read`). It must assert that each required secret is non-empty and that the App Store Connect API key authenticates — e.g. resolve the team and app record — then print only `OK` and never the key, issuer ID, or team ID. Follow the Loop/xDrip4iOS precedent for the secret set (ASC key ID / issuer ID / `.p8` contents, plus `match` password and a PAT if using a certificates repo). Retire the project-internal 1Password lane unless a maintainer-owned validation build is actually wanted; with no project-published binary there is nothing for it to sign.
- Signing Action Smoke (end-to-end keystore materialization) + op-load-signing-secrets composite action :: The Android design — a dispatch-only smoke that proves the composite action both loads and works, with umask 077, delete-before-create, an ERR trap, chmod 600, path-only logging, and caller-side `rm -f` under `if: always()` — is exactly the right shape and every one of those properties has an iOS counterpart. The mechanism changes from materializing a `.jks` to importing a certificate and provisioning profile into an ephemeral macOS keychain. :: REC: Composite action creating an ephemeral keychain, invoked by a `workflow_dispatch`-only `Signing Smoke` job whose only assertion is that `xcodebuild -showBuildSettings` resolves a signing identity and a matching profile for the bundle ID — asserted, never printed. Carry over every hygiene property: umask before write, delete-before-create, trap-on-error cleanup, 0600 modes, and a caller-side `if: always()` teardown (`security delete-keychain`), because composite actions still cannot register a post-job step. Add one iOS-only assertion the Android smoke had no reason to make: fail if the resolved entitlements request any capability not enabled on the signing team, so a fork user gets a clear preflight error instead of an opaque late signing failure.
- CodeRabbit "BLE Protocol Safety" custom check (mode: error) — SAFETY :: The intent transfers wholesale but roughly half the concrete rules are Android-GATT-specific and would be unreviewable or wrong on iOS. `GATT 133` does not exist. Bond loss surfaces differently. CoreBluetooth serializes nothing for you but the failure mode and the delegate contract differ, and iOS adds an entire failure class Android does not have: `CBCentralManager` state preservation and restoration, where the app is relaunched into the background with a restore identifier and must rebuild state without resuming arbitrary work. :: REC: Rewrite against CoreBluetooth and add the restoration rules explicitly: one outstanding peripheral operation at a time with an explicit timeout; every `CBPeripheral` delegate path handles `didDisconnectPeripheral`, `CBError.peerRemovedPairingInformation`, `CBError.connectionTimeout`, and `CBATTError.insufficientAuthentication`; `centralManager(_:willRestoreState:)` rebuilds from the restored peripherals dictionary and never assumes in-memory state survived; the manager is constructed with a stable `CBCentralManagerOptionRestoreIdentifierKey`. Then move what can be tested out of review entirely: byte-order, event-ID, and reassembly rules belong in replay tests over recorded frames, which run in the Simulator and CI where the radio does not.
- CodeRabbit "Medical Safety Review" custom check (mode: error) — SAFETY :: The rules are platform-independent and must survive verbatim: glucose stored and compared in canonical mg/dL, bounds 20–500, mmol/L display and input only, single conversion factor 18.0156, hard-fail on storing an mmol/L value as mg/dL (persisting 3.9 instead of ~70 silently suppresses low alerts), on inverted conversion, on out-of-range persisted values, and on any other factor. What must change is that on Android these invariants are enforced ONLY by an advisory reviewer — `fail_commit_status: false` means even a `mode: error` check does not block a merge. :: REC: All three, and treat the type-system version as the primary control. This is the single highest-value change the port can make: Swift's value types and access control let the mg/dL-canonical, bounds-checked invariant be a compile error rather than a reviewer's judgment. Keep the CodeRabbit check as defence in depth and keep its explicit non-violations so reviewers do not "fix" correct code — user-facing mmol/L display literals (3.9, 10.0, 22.2; ~1.1 and ~27.8 as the display equivalents of the bounds) and mmol/L input converted before storage are not bounds violations.
- CodeRabbit tool matrix and path_instructions :: Mechanism ports; contents do not. `detekt` is Kotlin-only. The path_instructions are written against Compose, Hilt, Room, EncryptedSharedPreferences, SQLCipher, and the runtime-plugin contract — and that last one is now wrong by decision: iOS drivers are compile-time-linked Swift modules, so `PLUGIN_API_VERSION` runtime-compatibility review is replaced by ordinary source/ABI compatibility. :: REC: Enable SwiftLint in place of detekt and rewrite path_instructions for: app/watch Swift (SwiftUI state ownership — `@State`/`@Observable`/`@Binding` correctness, `accessibilityIdentifier` for UI tests, structured concurrency and actor isolation, Keychain accessibility class, Data Protection class, no sensitive data in logs, plus the mg/dL rules verbatim); the driver API module (protocol stability, no app-layer imports, safety-limits documentation, and the read-only posture rule — no bolus, basal, pump-setting, or any therapeutic write primitive on any protocol); shipped driver modules (thin delegation, always forward safety limits, never a defaulted `SafetyLimits()`, lifecycle activate/deactivate/shutdown, monitoring-only); tests; and `.github/workflows/**` unchanged. Replace the `PLUGIN_API_VERSION` rule with an API-surface snapshot gate (below).
- Mobile security measures verification matrix :: Every row maps to a different iOS control, and two Android rows (SQLCipher, EncryptedSharedPreferences) were verified by "unit tests + CodeRabbit review" while two more (no sensitive data in logs, medical safety) had CodeRabbit as their ONLY verifier — against a non-blocking commit status. The port is the moment to make those executable. :: REC: Rebuild the matrix with checkable verifiers. Database encryption → SQLCipher (or Data Protection) plus a unit test asserting the file protection attribute; critically, the protection class must be `CompleteUntilFirstUserAuthentication` or `CompleteUnlessOpen`, never `NSFileProtectionComplete`, or background BLE writes fail while the device is locked — a real, testable invariant with no Android analogue. Token storage → Keychain with `kSecAttrAccessibleAfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly`, enforced by a CodeQL/Semgrep rule that fails on `kSecAttrAccessibleWhenUnlocked` or any non-`ThisDeviceOnly` class for pump/auth items, so pump credentials never sync to iCloud Keychain. Transport → a gate failing on `NSAllowsArbitraryLoads` or any ATS exception. Logging → a lint rule banning interpolation of pump serials/raw frames outside `#if DEBUG`, replacing pure review.
- DECISION: MobSF / artifact-level SAST declined, with its revisit trigger :: The Android rationale transfers almost intact — monitoring-only, read-only, not store-distributed, high false-positive tax, per-PR artifact build cost. But the iOS threat model shifts the calculus in one direction and the compensating control disappears in another: Android Lint covered manifest and network-security posture defensively, and iOS ships nothing equivalent, so declining artifact SAST on iOS leaves a wider gap than declining it on Android did. :: REC: Decline MobSF for the same stated reasons, and add an `Entitlements & Plist Guard` step inside the Static Analysis Gate. Deterministic, no baseline to maintain, no false positives: fail on `NSAllowsArbitraryLoads` or any ATS exception; fail if `get-task-allow` is true in a release configuration; assert the declared `UIBackgroundModes` are exactly the set the app actually needs; assert every required usage description string is present and non-placeholder (`NSBluetoothAlwaysUsageDescription`, `NSHealthShareUsageDescription`/`NSHealthUpdateUsageDescription` if HealthKit is used, and any location string if BLE scanning needs it); and fail if an entitlement is added that Apple grants only by out-of-band request — Critical Alerts being the specific case, since a fork-and-build model cannot obtain a per-user grant and the build would simply fail to sign for every user. Carry the revisit trigger verbatim: add artifact SAST if distribution ever changes or if any write/therapeutic surface is introduced.
- Adding security coverage for a new pump driver :: On Android a new driver is a runtime plugin whose Kotlin lands under `plugins/**` and is auto-covered by three filters. On iOS drivers are compile-time-linked modules, which removes the runtime API-version compatibility problem and replaces it with a static one — and simultaneously creates an enforcement opportunity Android does not have. :: REC: Auto-cover as before, and add a `Driver API Surface` check: snapshot the public API of the driver-protocol module (via `swift-api-digester` or a committed interface snapshot) and fail on any diff not accompanied by a lead-reviewed update. This makes the read-only posture — no bolus, no basal, no pump-setting write, no device command on any protocol — a mechanically enforced property rather than a review rule, which is the single strongest safety improvement available in this subsystem. Keep the one manual Android requirement: a driver introducing a new pairing or auth flow must add coverage in its own test suite; there is still no shared auth-flow harness.
- Local reproduction of each gate :: Every command changes, and three Android reproduction snippets are already documented incorrectly (SAST repro omits `tools/`, dep-scan repro omits `--no-ignore`, and there is no documented zizmor repro at all). Porting the docs verbatim would import those defects. :: REC: Use the script, and have CI call it. Cover: static analysis (CodeQL CLI with the pinned pack, or Semgrep `p/secrets`), dependency scan (pinned OSV-Scanner with the exact CI flags including `--no-ignore`), workflow lint (`actionlint -shellcheck=shellcheck`, with the documented caveat that it does NOT check the SHA-pin or composite-secrets guards), workflow security (`zizmor --config zizmor.yml --min-severity=medium .github/workflows/ .github/actions/` — the repro Android never documented), resolution drift, and CodeRabbit CLI pre-review. Note explicitly which gates cannot be reproduced on a machine without Xcode.
- Renovate coverage and lockfile regeneration :: Renovate's `github-actions` manager ports unchanged. The `gradle` manager is replaced by the `swift` manager over `Package.swift`. The Android `regen-gradle-lockfiles.yml` workaround exists because Renovate's container lacks the Android SDK; the identical problem exists on iOS because Renovate's container lacks a Swift toolchain and cannot regenerate `Package.resolved`. Separately, the Android repo's pip/go/tarball tool pins sit under no manager at all and are documented as silently rotting. :: REC: Do the first two. Keep the same tiering discipline: all `github-actions` updates including digest re-pins stay manual with `needs-review`; security-sensitive packages (crypto, cipher, keychain wrappers) stay manual; majors stay manual. Then close the Android gap by adding regex managers for the pinned actionlint, shellcheck, zizmor, OSV-Scanner, CodeQL pack, and — importantly — the pinned Xcode version, so the toolchain cannot drift or silently vanish from the runner image without a reviewed PR.
- Supply-chain surface of build-time code execution (new, no direct Android analogue) :: Gradle build scripts are arbitrary code too, but on iOS three distinct vectors execute arbitrary code inside a build that, under fork-and-build, has the user's signing credentials in its environment: `Package.swift` is executable Swift evaluated at resolve time, SPM build-tool and prebuild plugins run during build, and Xcode Run Script build phases execute during every build. On Android there was one project-held keystore; here there are N user-held signing identities, one per fork. :: REC: All three. Add a `Build Script & Plugin Guard` to the Workflow Lint job that greps `project.pbxproj` for `PBXShellScriptBuildPhase`, `Package.swift` for `.plugin(` and `binaryTarget(`, and fails on any hit not present in a committed, CODEOWNERS-owned allowlist. Require every `binaryTarget` to be checksum-pinned. In the fork-build workflow, keep signing secrets out of the environment of any job that compiles repo code where practical, and never grant them to a `pull_request`-triggered job. This is the gate that protects users' Apple Developer accounts, and it is the security control the Android CI never needed.

### SAFETY-CRITICAL (15)
- Glucose bounds are encoded ONLY as CI review policy, not as executable CI checks: canonical storage/comparison unit mg/dL, valid range 20–500 mg/dL, single allowed conversion factor 18.0156 mg/dL per mmol/L. Enforced by the CodeRabbit "Medical Safety Review" custom check (.coderabbit.yaml:210-234) and the {app,wear-device,watchface}/**/*.kt path_instructions. No Semgrep rule, unit test, or gradle check in this subsystem enforces them.
- Named failure mode with patient impact, verbatim from .coderabbit.yaml: persisting an mmol/L value as if it were mg/dL (e.g. storing 3.9 instead of ~70) SILENTLY SUPPRESSES LOW ALERTS. Also hard-fail: converting the wrong direction, permitting a stored mg/dL value outside 20–500 after conversion, or using any factor other than 18.0156.
- Explicit non-violations that must not be 'fixed': user-facing mmol/L display literals (3.9, 10.0, 22.2; test-path doc names ~1.1 and ~27.8 mmol/L as the display equivalents of the 20/500 bounds), and mmol/L input converted to mg/dL before storage.
- Monitoring-only, read-only posture is a safety and legal boundary enforced by review only: no auto-bolus, no bolus/basal/pump-setting write, no therapeutic device command may exist on any capability interface (SECURITY.md, .coderabbit.yaml pump-driver-api and shipped-driver path_instructions). SECURITY.md states a report proposing to add write capability will be declined.
- Introducing ANY write/therapeutic surface is a documented revisit trigger for adding APK/manifest SAST (docs/dev/security-testing.md:107-110) — i.e. the current security-gate design is calibrated to the read-only threat model and must be re-derived if that changes.
- BLE protocol correctness is gated only by the CodeRabbit "BLE Protocol Safety" check: GATT ops serialized and timed out; connection state machine handles unexpected disconnect, bond loss, GATT 133; Little Endian for the Tandem protocol; event type IDs match the protocol spec; multi-packet reassembly honors the documented idle timeout. No CI tool verifies any of this.
- Never log raw pump data, pump serials, or auth material at INFO level (DEBUG only). Verified solely by CodeRabbit review per the security-testing.md matrix.
- SafetyLimits must always be forwarded to the underlying BLE driver/parser; `SafetyLimits()` defaults are forbidden in shipped drivers. Review-only enforcement.
- CodeRabbit `fail_commit_status: false` means the three `mode: error` custom checks — including Medical Safety Review and BLE Protocol Safety — do NOT block a merge via GitHub status. The only hard-blocking safety control is human CODEOWNERS review on /plugins/pump-driver-api/, /plugins/shipped/tandem/, /plugins/shipped/medtronic/.
- CodeRabbit skips PRs authored by renovate/dependabot/release/github-actions bots, so dependency-bump PRs receive no medical-safety or BLE review from that layer.
- Dependency Scan Gate PASSES without scanning on any PR that does not touch a dependency-relevant path; a newly disclosed CVE in an unchanged dependency is caught only by the Monday 06:00 UTC cron or a manual dispatch.
- Instrumented Tests (emulator) — which executes the Room migration test and Compose/espresso UI tests — is deliberately NOT a required check, so a PR can merge with those tests red.
