================================================================================
## Docs site, legal, and community policy surface  [docs-and-policy]
================================================================================

### iOS REQUIREMENTS (46)
- The published docs tree must use one _meta.json per section directory with the schema {"title": string, "pages": [string]}, entries as basenames without extension, the array order as the rendered nav order, and no root docs/_meta.json.
- CI must fail a documentation PR when a page under a section directory lacks title: or description: frontmatter, is present on disk but absent from its section's pages array, or is listed in a pages array but absent from disk.
- CI must fail a documentation PR containing an internal link that resolves to nothing, evaluated against the merged multi-repo website namespace rather than this repo alone.
- iOS documentation pages must not share page basenames with the Android repository's pages; platform-specific content must live in platform-scoped section directories.
- A push to main touching docs/** must dispatch a docs-updated repository event to the website repo using a GitHub App token minted with no GITHUB_TOKEN scopes, with a manual re-dispatch available to force a rebuild after a failed run.
- A user must be able to go from a fresh fork to a working app on their own iPhone using only the published build-and-install page: Apple Developer Program membership, an App Store Connect API key, a documented set of repository secrets, one workflow run, and a TestFlight invite.
- The build-and-install page must state the 90-day TestFlight build expiry and the exact action required to refresh it before it lapses.
- The app must surface its own build expiry date and source tag in Settings so a user can see staleness without consulting TestFlight.
- The project must never hold, request, or transmit any user's Apple signing material; all signing credentials must live only in the user's own fork's repository secrets, and a leaked credential must compromise only that user's own Apple account.
- The build pipeline must produce a signed, TestFlight-uploadable build from a fork in which no project-owned secret is present.
- The app must ship an in-app license viewer reachable from Settings, with distinct Loading, Loaded and Unavailable states, full text selection, preserved scroll position across rotation and scene restoration, and no network requirement.
- The license viewer must display, in order: the project license notice, the third-party source-lineage document, one section per redistributed-component license family, the bundled license texts, and the complete GPL-3.0 text.
- License texts and the GPL must be rendered as plain text and never as markdown, and repository-relative links in any displayed document must be reduced to their label text while scheme-bearing targets including mailto: are preserved.
- The shipped license assets must be generated from the resolved dependency graph on every build, covering every target that ships in a binary including the watch app and any widget extension; committed pre-generated assets must not be accepted.
- The build must fail — not warn — when the resolved component list is empty, when a component has no SPDX identifier and no classification entry, when a license family has no bundled text file, when a whole-file source document is blank, or when README.md has no `## License` heading.
- The build must fail when a dependency's license is not on the checked-in allowlist; EPL-1.0 must not be allowlisted, and an EPL-licensed transitive must be excluded at the dependency with a test proving the affected feature still works.
- Every per-package license exception must be pinned to an exact version and carry a written reason, so a version bump re-raises the judgement rather than inheriting it.
- An automated test must assert byte-for-byte equality between each shipped license asset and its on-disk source, must fail on any component-document heading outside the declared SPDX and non-SPDX heading sets, and must assert a named stable witness component and a non-empty upstream-notices block.
- Editing README.md, LICENSE, docs/THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md or docs/licenses/** must invalidate cached test results and trigger the application build gate; every other docs change must skip it.
- Every in-scope Swift, shell and workflow file must carry the SPDX identifier GPL-3.0-only and the copyright line naming the individual holder; ported upstream trees must be excluded from that stamp and must instead carry their own upstream notice in-file, specifically including Apache-2.0 and the Particle Industries copyright on the EC-JPAKE source.
- A published acknowledgments page must state, per upstream project, whether its code was studied and reimplemented or copied and ported, and must name the license and any explicit relicensing permission relied on.
- A repo-internal, unpublished provenance record must state, per Swift module, which Android source file and commit it derives from, or that it was reimplemented from protocol documentation only.
- The published medical disclaimer must state that no therapeutic write surface exists anywhere, that no capability protocol exposes an insulin-delivery primitive, and that permitted device-management operations are exactly CGM calibration, BLE pair and unpair, and connect and disconnect.
- The published alert-delivery warning must enumerate every iOS-specific condition that alert delivery depends on, and must state that app alerts are a supplement to the pump's and CGM's own alerts, never a replacement, and that device alerts must remain enabled.
- No product surface may claim that alerts are guaranteed, undismissable, or capable of overriding the user's own notification, Focus, or mute settings.
- The published README must carry an iOS-specific per-device verification table including a 'validated on iOS hardware by / on date' column, and the medical disclaimer must defer to that table; no device status may be inherited from the Android table.
- Onboarding must present a safety-acknowledgement page that cannot be skipped or swiped past, requires an explicit 'I Understand' action, and shows all five disclaimer cards including the warning never to calculate an insulin dose from a photo carb estimate; users who previously completed onboarding must be re-run through it whenever the disclaimer changes.
- The published privacy document must enumerate every outbound connection by name, state that each is user-chosen, and state that with no backend configured the app performs local BLE monitoring, on-device storage and local threshold alerts with zero network calls.
- Any error-monitoring integration must compile with an empty DSN in every pipeline-produced build, must fail the build outright if a DSN reaches CI, and must transmit no glucose, health, identifier, credential, device-serial, database or HTTP-body data.
- Credentials must be stored in the Keychain with a device-only, after-first-unlock accessibility class; health data must be stored under a file-protection class no weaker than complete-until-first-user-authentication; the watch must store no credentials and must make no network requests.
- Security reports must have two private channels and no public issue path, and the policy must classify a BLE parsing defect that produces a wrong glucose, IoB or dosing-history value as a report for that channel rather than an ordinary bug.
- The security policy must pre-decline, in writing, any report proposing that the absent therapeutic-write capability be added.
- Every commit must carry a valid Signed-off-by trailer, enforced server-side with no exemption tier and a one-remediation-commit allowance, with no CLA and no relicensing rights granted.
- Pull requests must be blocked when AI attribution appears in commit trailers, in added source lines, or in the PR description, using the same three-severity ladder and anchored bot allowlist, with .swift included in the scanned extension set.
- Required pull-request checks must cover, in substance: build and unit tests on iOS and watchOS Simulator destinations, Swift static analysis with security rules that demonstrably parse Swift, dependency CVE scanning over the committed dependency lockfile, license attribution generation, AI attribution, and workflow lint plus workflow security scanning.
- The Swift static-analysis gate must fail closed if the scanner errors, and must fail if it reports zero scanned Swift files.
- Fork-originated pull requests must run every required check with no maintainer action, and no privileged check may check out pull-request code into a writable working tree.
- A driver contribution must declare which capability protocols it implements from the read-only set, must discard and log any reading outside SafetyLimits rather than returning, emitting or persisting it, must include parsing and limits-validation tests, and must name who will validate it on physical hardware.
- The three cross-repo safety constants — 18.0156, the 20 to 500 mg/dL validity bound, and the 1199145600-second Tandem epoch offset — must each exist as exactly one Swift definition, be documented on a single contract page listing every consuming site, and be guarded by a test that fails when a duplicate or divergent definition appears.
- Glucose must be stored and compared in mg/dL at every persisted field, bound, threshold, alert comparison, dedup key and safety check; mmol/L must exist only at display and input boundaries, converted with the single sanctioned factor and rounded last.
- The app must vendor a monotonic CONTRACT_VERSION integer and a byte-for-byte-pinned OpenAPI document, must treat an absent or unparseable backend compatibility signal as absent rather than compatible, must fail open on read and monitoring paths with a one-time dismissible warning, and must refuse any future safety-critical write when backend compatibility is unknown.
- Contract tests must assert the presence of every nullable consumed field whose absence would silently degrade a safety surface, explicitly including the Nightscout pump-event insulin-units field whose absence drops the event and understates IoB.
- The repository must ship a code of conduct with a reporting address distinct from the security address, a governance stub delegating to the organisation-level document, a CODEOWNERS file requiring lead review on driver, workflow, policy and release-configuration changes, and pull-request and issue templates including a hardware-validation field.
- Documentation-only pull requests must receive a docs label and a Documentation changelog section without triggering the application build gate, except when they touch one of the four license-bearing paths.
- The watch setup page must state that the watch app installs automatically with the phone app, that a complication must be added manually to an Apple-provided face, and that watchOS provides no custom watch face and no equivalent of pushing a face from the phone.
- An in-app diagnostics surface must report Bluetooth manager state and authorization, Background App Refresh status, Low Power Mode, and the last-seen-advertisement and last-successful-pump-read timestamps, so troubleshooting documentation has something actionable to point at.

### IMPOSSIBLE (7)
- A project-published, project-signed installable binary (app-release.apk from the topmost non-pre-release, and the dev-latest debug pre-release) :: Apple permits installation only via the App Store, TestFlight, ad-hoc or enterprise provisioning bound to a specific team and enumerated devices, or a development build signed for a registered device. There is no signed-artifact-on-a-releases-page model at any tier. A TestFlight group belongs to exactly one App Store Connect team, external testing requires Apple's Beta App Review, and the project has deliberately chosen no App Store listing — which is also what keeps GPL-3.0-only workable, since the App Store's terms conflict with GPLv3's anti-DRM and installation-information provisions. The Android two-track model (signed release APK for users, debug APK from a dev-latest pre-release for developers) therefore has no counterpart on either track. :: ALT: The fork-and-build model already chosen, following Loop and xDrip4iOS: the project publishes SOURCE at signed git tags, and each user's own fork runs a GitHub Actions workflow that signs with the user's own Apple Developer account and uploads to that user's own internal TestFlight (up to 100 internal testers, 90-day build expiry, no Beta App Review). The docs replace 'download the topmost release without the Pre-release label' with 'run your fork's release workflow against tag vX.Y.Z', and the release-vs-dev-latest distinction becomes tag-build vs main-branch-build in the user's own fork. This is also strictly stronger for GPL compliance: every user provably holds the corresponding source and their own signing key.
- Custom watch face (the watchface module, Watch Face Format, and the phone-pushes-the-face Watch Face Push API) :: watchOS has no third-party watch faces. Apple exposes no public API to author, install, push, or replace a face; there is no format analogous to Watch Face Format, no counterpart to androidx.wear.watchface:watchface-push, and no counterpart to the Wear OS 5 versus 6 branching the Android page documents. This is not a permissions or entitlement gap that a request could close — the surface does not exist for developers at any tier. Everything downstream of it disappears too: the separate watchface-release.apk, the two-variant watchface build (digitalFull and analogMechanical) that the Android attribution generator resolves as two of its four AttributionSources, and the entire ADB-install-the-face workflow. :: ALT: WidgetKit complications in the accessoryCircular, accessoryCorner, accessoryRectangular and accessoryInline families, which the user manually adds to an Apple-provided face, plus a Smart Stack widget with relevance hinting, plus the full watch app for the richer view. Every data element the Android face renders is preserved — glucose, trend arrow up/down/flat, IoB, time — as is the ~5-minute glucose-interval cadence, the '--' staleness threshold, and the strictly read-only posture. What is genuinely lost: control of the whole face surface, any guarantee of placement (the user chooses the face and the slot), and guaranteed refresh cadence (complication timeline reloads are budgeted by the system). The docs must state all three losses rather than implying parity.
- ADB-based install, sideloading, and the separate watch-app install workflow :: iOS and watchOS expose no adb equivalent, no unknown-sources permission, no Developer Options unlocked by tapping Build number seven times, no Debug over Wi-Fi or Bluetooth pairing that surfaces an <ip>:<port>, and no way to install an app onto the watch independently of the phone. INSTALL_FAILED_USER_RESTRICTED, the work-device policy-block case, and the 'Wear OS forbids APK download from the watch browser' constraint the Android docs call the hardest step in the whole product all have no analogue. :: ALT: The watch app is embedded in the phone app's bundle and installs automatically when the user installs the phone app from TestFlight. The entire Android watch-install page collapses to 'accept the TestFlight invite, then long-press your watch face, tap Edit, and add the GlycemicGPT complication.' This is a strict simplification and the docs should say so explicitly — the single hardest documented step in the Android product is removed. For developers, Xcode's Devices and Simulators window installs directly to a connected iPhone, which is the nearest thing to the adb path and belongs in the developer section, not the user-facing install page.
- In-app 'Check for updates' that downloads and installs a newer build :: iOS forbids an application from fetching and executing code that was not signed into its bundle; there is no counterpart to downloading an APK from github.com or objects.githubusercontent.com and installing it, and no in-place upgrade path an app can trigger. The Android page's related claims — no auto-update, a stale-version banner, and an in-place APK upgrade that preserves settings, login and pairing — are all either impossible or inverted, since TestFlight updates automatically and installs are managed by the system. :: ALT: TestFlight delivers updates automatically once the user's fork uploads a new build. The app may at most compare its own embedded source tag against the upstream repository's latest tag and display a non-actionable banner: 'a newer version is available — re-run your fork's build workflow.' PRIVACY.md's fourth outbound connection changes character accordingly: it stops being an install path and becomes, at most, an explicitly user-initiated version check that downloads nothing. The app should additionally surface its own TestFlight build expiry date, which is a real staleness signal the Android app has no need for.
- Guaranteed or undismissable alert delivery, and the Critical Alerts entitlement :: UNNotificationInterruptionLevel.critical requires the com.apple.developer.usernotifications.critical-alerts entitlement, which Apple grants only by reviewed request against a specific App Store Connect app record, and which is not realistically obtainable for a user-built, non-App-Store, explicitly-unofficial app — and every user's fork would need its own grant against its own bundle ID. Separately, iOS has no foreground service: there is no mechanism to keep a process alive indefinitely. BGTaskScheduler runs at the system's discretion and cannot guarantee a 5-minute cadence; Core Bluetooth background delivery is event-driven only; and CBCentralManager state restoration hands back the peripherals and scan state but explicitly cannot resume arbitrary work, so an app relaunched into the background must reconstruct its own context and may be terminated again. Android's battery-optimization exemption, which the Android docs present as the fix for a dropping background link, has no user-facing counterpart at all. :: ALT: A layered, honestly-described alerting posture: .timeSensitive interruption level (breaks through Focus, but not through the user muting the app or disabling its notifications), a Live Activity while a pump session is active so glucose stays visible on the Lock Screen and in the Dynamic Island, an audible in-app alarm while the app is foreground, the bluetooth-central background mode with CBCentralManagerOptionRestoreIdentifierKey so the OS relaunches the app on peripheral events, and a watch-side complication as an independent surface. MEDICAL-DISCLAIMER Critical Warning #4 must be made STRICTER on iOS and must never be weakened: the supplement-not-replacement framing and the instruction to keep the pump's and CGM's own alerts enabled are the actual safety mechanism, and the docs must not imply the app can be relied on as a primary alarm.
- Runtime-loaded plugin contribution path (plugins/example/) :: iOS forbids loading executable code that was not signed into the app bundle at build time. There is no counterpart to DexClassLoader, dlopen of a downloaded binary is rejected at load, and any mechanism that fetched and executed a driver at runtime would be both a signing violation and a code-injection surface in an app handling glucose data. This is a settled platform rule, not a v1 scope cut, and is already reflected in the product decision that drivers are compile-time-linked Swift modules. :: ALT: Every driver is a compile-time-linked SwiftPM target under Sources/Drivers/. The CONTRIBUTING step describing runtime-loaded plugins as a separate advanced path not compiled into official builds is DELETED rather than adapted, and the docs should state the tradeoff plainly: adding a new device on iOS requires a source change, a merged PR, and a rebuild by each user, not a dropped-in plugin. One mitigating property of the distribution model is that a user who wants an unmerged driver can already build their own fork with it — which is the same affordance the plugin path provided, arrived at differently.
- Battery-optimization exemption as a documented, user-performable action :: iOS has no per-app battery-optimization allowlist, no Samsung-style 'Sleeping apps' list, and no user-facing setting that grants an app durable background execution. The Android docs make this exemption a prerequisite in two separate published pages (install.md and cant-pair-pump.md) because without it the background pump link drops; there is nothing on iOS a user can toggle that provides the equivalent guarantee. The related '<5%/day battery impact' figure is an Android measurement of an Android background model and cannot be carried over. :: ALT: Document what the user CAN control and be explicit about what they cannot: enable Background App Refresh for the app, keep Low Power Mode off, and do not swipe the app away in the app switcher (which suspends Core Bluetooth state restoration until the next manual launch, and is therefore the exact opposite of the Android 'force-stop then reopen' remedy). Pair this with an in-app diagnostics screen surfacing CBManagerState, Bluetooth authorization, Background App Refresh status, Low Power Mode and a last-seen-advertisement timestamp, since iOS hides all of it. Replace the battery figure with a measured iOS number gathered on the maintainer's hardware, or publish none at all — do not inherit the Android one.

### NEEDS REDESIGN (19)
- Cross-repo shared docs namespace (relative links leave this repo) :: The website merges this repo's docs/ tree with the platform repo's into ONE namespace; roughly eight relative links per published page (../get-started.md and its anchors, ../troubleshooting/bg-not-updating.md, ../concepts/what-this-software-is-and-isnt.md, ../concepts/privacy.md, ../daily-use/dashboard.md, ./connecting-medtronic.md) resolve only post-merge. Adding a third repo creates direct basename collisions: android-unofficial and ios-unofficial would both contribute mobile/install.md, troubleshooting/cant-pair-pump.md and daily-use/connecting-medtronic-pump.md into the same tree. Nothing in either repo detects the collision, and there is no link checker anywhere. :: REC: Option 1. Use docs/ios/ and docs/apple-watch/ as the platform-specific sections, keep adr/concepts/contract/daily-use/dev/troubleshooting as shared-shape sections with iOS-unique page basenames, and route every cross-repo link through a small stable anchor set the platform repo owns (get-started, concepts/privacy, concepts/what-this-software-is-and-isnt, daily-use/dashboard). Add a link check that runs in the website repo against the MERGED tree — it cannot be run correctly from inside this repo — plus an in-repo check for links that stay local. Pick one canonical org/slug before copying any absolute link forward; the Android repo currently carries six inconsistent slugs across its policy docs.
- docs/mobile/install.md → the iOS build-and-install contract :: Nothing in the Android page survives. There is no APK, no 'topmost release without the Pre-release label', no dev-latest debug pre-release, no 'Allow from this source' toggle, no adb install, no INSTALL_FAILED_USER_RESTRICTED, and no in-place upgrade that preserves settings and pairing. The Android page's 'no auto-update; stale-version banner' statement is false on iOS because TestFlight updates automatically. The distribution model itself is the page's subject and it has changed completely: the user builds and signs their own binary. :: REC: Option 3. The page must cover, in order: prerequisites (Apple Developer Program membership, an App Store Connect API key, a GitHub account, iOS 17+/watchOS 10+ hardware); fork the repo; create an app record and choose a bundle-ID prefix; add the documented set of repository secrets (Issuer ID, Key ID, .p8 contents, Team ID, bundle-ID prefix, app name); run the Build and Upload to TestFlight workflow; accept the TestFlight invite on the iPhone; the 90-day TestFlight build expiry and the re-run needed before it lapses. Then onboarding (server URL then sign-in), then pairing. Preserve the Android page's genuinely portable content: the reachable-platform-URL requirement (http://<lan-ip>:3000 local or https://domain), the Tandem one-BLE-connection-at-a-time rule and unpair t:connect first, and the 'if the dashboard is still empty after 5 minutes' troubleshooting threshold. Replace the '<5%/day battery impact' figure with a measured iOS figure or omit it — do not inherit it. Delete the 'Today only Android is supported; iOS is on the roadmap' statement from the Android page in the same coordinated release.
- docs/mobile/wear-os.md → docs/apple-watch/setup.md :: The Android page's entire subject has no counterpart. There is no wear-device companion APK and no watchface APK, no Wear OS 5 vs 6 branching, no Watch Face Push API, no ADB-install-the-face path, and no 'tap Build number 7×, enable Debug over Wi-Fi, adb connect <ip>:<port>' workflow. On iOS the watch app is embedded in the phone app's bundle and installs automatically with it. :: REC: Option 3. The standalone page must state: the watch app installs automatically with the phone app from the same TestFlight build (no separate install); there is no custom watch face on watchOS, so the user manually adds a GlycemicGPT complication to an Apple-provided face (long-press face → Edit → tap a complication slot → choose GlycemicGPT); the Smart Stack widget is a second surface; which complication families are supported (accessoryCircular, accessoryCorner, accessoryRectangular, accessoryInline). Preserve the portable content: glucose, trend arrow (up/down/flat), IoB and time are rendered; refresh follows the ~5-minute glucose reading interval; the display shows '--' for glucose more than 5 minutes stale after fresh setup; the surface is explicitly read-only. Add that WatchConnectivity delivery is best-effort and complication timeline budget is system-managed, so the watch can be staler than the phone. State plainly that this page replaces the hardest step in the Android product with an automatic one.
- docs/troubleshooting/cant-pair-pump.md :: Three of the Android page's five prerequisites are Android-only. iOS requires NO Location permission for BLE scanning (only NSBluetoothAlwaysUsageDescription), has no user-visible battery-optimization exemption and no Samsung 'Sleeping apps' list, and has no force-stop affordance — the closest, swiping the app away in the app switcher, also tears down Core Bluetooth state restoration, which makes the Android 'force-stop then reopen' remedy actively harmful advice on iOS. The Android 11+ (API 30+) requirement becomes an iOS version floor. :: REC: Option 2 — the diagnostics screen is what makes the page actionable, because iOS hides everything the Android page tells a user to inspect. Preserve verbatim: the ordering rule that the app must be scanning BEFORE the pump starts advertising; the pump 6-digit code timing out after a couple of minutes; the Tandem one-BLE-connection rule and unpair-t:connect-first; pump within a couple of metres; both batteries healthy; metal or magnetic phone cases as a documented cause; pump BLE flake remedied by a power cycle and a 10-second wait; and Tandem Mobi as protocol-compatible but unverified on hardware, at your own risk. Preserve the log-redaction warning before posting to Discord in full: emails, bearer/API tokens, auth headers, account IDs, pump serials and BLE device identifiers replaced with [REDACTED] or DM'd to a maintainer. Replace the force-stop remedy with 'toggle Bluetooth off and on, then reopen the app — do NOT swipe the app away'. Replace battery optimization with Background App Refresh on and Low Power Mode off.
- docs/daily-use/connecting-medtronic-pump.md :: The documented pairing mechanism depends on the PHONE advertising as a BLE peripheral with the exact local name 'Mobile 000001' while the PUMP initiates — advertise-and-wait, inverted from Tandem. On iOS, CBPeripheralManager includes CBAdvertisementDataLocalNameKey only while the app is in the FOREGROUND; once backgrounded, iOS moves advertising into the overflow area and drops the local name entirely, so a backgrounded iPhone is not discoverable by the name the pump looks for. The Android page's 'pairing is remembered; reconnect is automatic when back in range' claim therefore splits into two different guarantees on iOS. :: REC: Options 1 and 2 together: document initial pairing as foreground-and-screen-on, and document background reconnection as best-effort under the bluetooth-peripheral background mode, with the page stating the limitation rather than implying parity. Do NOT choose option 3 — parity is the stated product goal. Preserve: MiniMed 680G/770G/780G scope, explicitly beta and read-only; the contrast with CareLink cloud sync as a separate unrelated path; the waiting → connecting → connected flow states; one-phone-at-a-time with removal from MiniMed Mobile first; the read surface (sensor glucose, IoB, basal active rate, bolus history, reservoir, battery); the documented limitation that SmartGuard auto-basal micro-boluses are not yet fully attributed; and the IoB-as-the-pump-exposes-it caveat subject to display rounding. Preserve all five named failure strings and add an iOS-specific sixth for Bluetooth permission not granted; the existing 'This phone can't act as a Bluetooth accessory.' string maps cleanly onto the iOS foreground/background constraint.
- docs/mobile/status-icons.mdx content semantics :: Two of the three status icons change meaning. iOS has no persistent foreground service, so a blue solid 'Connected' state describes a CBPeripheral link the OS may suspend at any moment without user-visible signal — the icon can be truthful and stale simultaneously. Backend reachability and watch reachability also become distinct questions on iOS in a way the Wear OS Data Layer masked. :: REC: Option 1, plus an explicit 'last successful pump read' timestamp in the status row — this is the single most valuable addition, because it is the only element that cannot silently lie while the app is suspended. Reject option 3: the Android doc's design rule that sync ('is my data uploaded') and reachability ('can I reach the server right now') answer different questions and must not look alike is load-bearing and gets MORE important on iOS. Preserve the colour vocabulary (blue #2563eb solid connected, amber #d97706 transitional, red #dc2626 error or explicitly-resting-not-error), the rule that sync and reachability icons are hidden entirely in backend-optional local mode, the at-most-one-pump-brand-mark rule (Tandem or Medtronic) plus data-source marks, unbranded drivers showing no mark, and the horizontally-scrolling mark row.
- generateDependencyAttribution — the component-list generator :: There is no app.cash.licensee for Swift. Package.resolved records pins and revisions but no license metadata, SwiftPM emits no per-target license report, and many Swift packages ship a LICENSE file with no machine-readable declaration at all. The Android task's four-AttributionSource fan-out over release runtime classpaths has no analogue either. :: REC: Option 2. Reject option 3: LicensePlist does not fail the build on an unclassified component, cannot enforce the no-empty-component-list rule, and has no policy allowlist — the fail-closed behavior IS the requirement here, not the file generation. The generator must reproduce every Android failure mode as a hard error: an empty component list ('would ship empty, which reads as this app redistributes nothing'); a component with no detectable SPDX identifier and no entry in the unmapped or unreproduced maps, named by package identity; a license family with no docs/licenses/<id>.txt, listing every missing family; and any checkout it cannot read. Resolution must be strict, never lenient — a failure breaks the build rather than silently dropping a NOTICE. It must cover EVERY target that ships in a binary (phone app, watch app, widget/complication extension, and every driver target), not just the phone app, because the Android pipeline's lesson is that five of six reproduced NOTICE files were reachable only through the watch module.
- NOTICE harvesting (Apache-2.0 §4(d) discharge) :: The Android implementation opens each resolved .jar/.aar as a zip and matches an anchored (META-INF/)?NOTICE(\.(txt|md))? regex, additionally descending into an .aar's nested classes.jar. SwiftPM dependencies are source checkouts on disk, not archives, so the mechanism becomes a filesystem walk — but the anchoring semantics must survive translation or the discharge becomes wrong. :: REC: Option 1 with option 2 as an explicit, reasoned escape hatch. The load-bearing rule to preserve is why the Android regex is fully anchored: a NOTICE nested under a vendored dependency inside a checkout belongs to THAT dependency and is its obligation, not this project's — a naive recursive find would over-attribute. Preserve the rest of the behavior exactly: multiple notices within one package sorted by path and joined with a blank line; empty or whitespace-only notices dropped; an unreadable checkout raising a hard error rather than silently dropping attribution; the notices map sorted by package identity; and a named stable witness package asserted by the drift test so a silent regression to zero notices is impossible.
- Licensee allowlist policy → a Package.resolved license gate :: Licensee's allow() / allowUrl() / allowDependency(coordinates, because:) DSL has no SwiftPM analogue, and the Android policy's most important content is a set of DELIBERATE judgements that must not be lost in translation — particularly the intentional omission of EPL-1.0 and the rule that a version bump re-raises rather than inherits an exception. :: REC: Option 1 — one policy file at the repo root so the phone, watch and driver targets cannot disagree, exactly as the Android root build.gradle.kts declares it once for three modules. Carry forward as first-class, with the reasoning attached: EPL-1.0 stays OFF the allowlist because the FSF considers it incompatible with GPLv3 for a combined distributed work, and any EPL-licensed package must be excluded AT THE DEPENDENCY with a test proving the feature still works without it — never allowlisted and never handled by lowering a threshold. Allow the project's own licence URL (gnu.org gpl-3.0.txt) the same way. Every per-package exception is pinned to an exact version with a written reason, so a bump re-raises the judgement. Reject option 3: it produces no PR-blocking gate.
- generateLicenseAssets — the five shipped assets :: There is no androidComponents.onVariants hook, no assets/ directory, and no per-variant asset source to null-check. The Android task's safety property — that a variant which lost its asset source fails loudly rather than shipping a binary with no license text — has to be reconstructed from different primitives. :: REC: Option 1 for SPM targets and option 2 for the app target; explicitly REJECT option 3, because committing generated assets breaks 'documents are the single source of truth', lets them go stale between dependency bumps, and turns the drift test into a tautology. Keep all five files and their exact names: project_license_notice.md (the README `## License` section including its heading, verbatim, up to the next `## `), third_party_licenses.md (docs/THIRD_PARTY_LICENSES.md whole), gpl-3.0.txt (repo-root LICENSE whole), runtime_dependencies.md and runtime_dependency_licenses.txt. Keep the rule that nothing is rewritten for display — normalisation belongs to the UI — so every asset stays an exact substring of its source and the drift test remains a plain equality check. Keep both hard failures: any whole-file source that is blank, and a README with no `## License` heading. Replace the per-variant checkNotNull with a per-configuration check covering Debug, Release and every fork's signing configuration, so no build path can ever ship a blank license screen.
- docs/dev/signing-and-secrets.md — the signing model :: The model inverts completely. Android keeps ONE project keystore and its passwords in 1Password, exposes exactly one GitHub secret (a read-only 1Password service-account token), and CI signs a project-owned APK. On iOS the project has NO signing identity at all — every user signs with their own Apple Developer account in their own fork. The 1Password vault, the op:// reference layout, the file-attachment-vs-text-field split, and the env-var contract into signingConfigs all become irrelevant. :: REC: Option 2. Automatic signing driven by an App Store Connect API key (Issuer ID, Key ID, base64 .p8), plus Team ID and a user-chosen bundle-ID prefix — six to eight repository secrets total, with no certificate, no provisioning profile and no private key committed anywhere in the repo or held by the project. Reject option 3 outright: Xcode Cloud is bound to a single Apple Developer account and its workflows do not fork, which is architecturally incompatible with the distribution model. Option 1 is viable but adds a second repo and an encryption passphrase per user for no gain under automatic signing. The rewritten page must state three guarantees plainly: the project never holds, requests, or transmits any user's Apple signing material; a leaked App Store Connect key compromises only that user's own account; and rotation is a one-secret change in the user's own fork.
- CONTRIBUTING — device-driver contribution path :: Both Android paths disappear. plugins/shipped/<device>/ Gradle modules become compile-time-linked SwiftPM targets, and the runtime-loaded plugins/example/ path is forbidden by iOS. The Android page also assumes a contributor can validate their own driver, which is false here: the lead has no iPhone and no Apple Watch, and Core Bluetooth does not exist in the Simulator. :: REC: Option 1 — a target is enough isolation, and a package per device multiplies Package.resolved churn and license-generator surface for no benefit. Preserve verbatim: the read-only capability set as Swift protocols (GLUCOSE_SOURCE, INSULIN_SOURCE, PUMP_STATUS, BGM_SOURCE, CALIBRATION_TARGET, BOLUS_CATEGORY_PROVIDER) with nothing else declarable; the hard SafetyLimits rule that a reading outside the limits must be DISCARDED — not returned, not emitted, not persisted — and the rejection LOGGED with the violated limit; unit tests required, especially on parsing and limits validation; the refusal to merge PRs introducing therapeutic write primitives and the refusal of contributions whose intent is to enable a device-control fork. ADD a step the Android page has no need for and which is now mandatory: a driver PR must name who will validate it on physical hardware and on which device, because Simulator validation cannot exercise BLE at all. Ship a simulated driver so parser correctness stays testable without hardware.
- CONTRIBUTING — setup, branching, commits, pre-submit checklist :: Prerequisites, build commands and local gate reproduction are entirely different toolchain. JDK 17 + Android SDK 35/36 + paired phone and watch emulators become macOS + Xcode + Simulator runtimes; ./gradlew assembleDebug / testDebugUnitTest / lintDebug become xcodebuild invocations. :: REC: Option 2 — the Android page's most useful property is that a contributor can reproduce every gate locally, and a wrapper is what preserves it across a much noisier toolchain. Prerequisites become macOS 15+, Xcode 16+, a Swift 6 toolchain, and iOS 18 / watchOS 11 Simulator runtimes. Quick start becomes fork, clone, add upstream, install the commit-msg hook (cp .githooks/commit-msg .git/hooks/ or git config core.hooksPath .githooks), then build and test both schemes. Branching, commits and the checklist port unchanged: develop and main, ALL contributor PRs target develop and never main, branch prefixes feat/ fix/ docs/ refactor/ ci/, Conventional Commits driving release-please with feat/fix/perf/docs/refactor/ci changelog-visible and chore/test hidden, and the pre-submit checklist (tests pass, lint clean with no new warnings, no hardcoded secrets, new functionality has tests, Conventional Commit format, every commit signed off, branch up to date with develop). ADD one line: Core Bluetooth does not exist in the Simulator, so any BLE-touching PR must state whether it was validated on hardware or against the simulated driver. Ship the .github/PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE.md and issue forms the Android repo is missing, with a hardware-validation field.
- CONTRIBUTING — required CI checks and fork-PR handling :: Five required checks map to different tools, and one maps to nothing. Semgrep's p/kotlin and p/java rulesets provide ZERO Swift coverage — porting the Security Scan Gate by name would produce a permanently green gate that scans nothing, which is worse than having no gate. The Android Gate's Gradle build becomes xcodebuild on macOS runners at roughly a 10× minute multiplier, so the paths-filter fan-out stops being an optimisation and becomes a cost requirement. Fork PRs also stop being merely a contribution path and become the primary distribution mechanism. :: REC: Option 2, satisfying substance rather than tool names. Concretely: **iOS Gate** — xcodebuild test on an iOS Simulator destination and a watchOS Simulator destination plus xcodebuild build for both schemes, gated behind a dorny/paths-filter fan-out with an always-running aggregation job that fails closed, exactly as android.yml does. **Security Scan Gate** — Semgrep p/swift and p/secrets (Semgrep's own registry supports Swift; the Kotlin/Java packs do not apply at all), plus swiftlint --strict with a security-focused custom rule set, plus a canary assertion that the scan actually parsed Swift files rather than reporting zero targets. **Dependency Scan Gate** — OSV-Scanner over the committed Package.resolved (OSV parses SwiftPM manifests), same fail-on-any-known-CVE policy, same osv-scanner.toml suppression file requiring a written reason and an ignoreUntil. **Attribution Check** — unchanged, including its pull_request_target design that never checks out PR code. **Workflow Lint / Workflow Security** — actionlint + shellcheck + SHA-pin guard, and zizmor at Medium+ with a rationale-per-entry allowlist and never a lowered threshold; both are language-agnostic and port as-is. Add an **Attribution Gate** for the license generator. Newly needed and absent from Android: an IPA/entitlements posture check is the analogue of the MobSF decision the Android repo declined — and its own stated revisit trigger (a write surface, or store distribution) does not fire here, so declining it again is consistent. Fork PRs must run every required check with no maintainer action.
- ADR 0002 / ADR 0003 — tolerant reader and contract-drift strategy :: The client half of ADR 0002 is stated in terms of Moshi. Swift Codable matches Moshi on the property that matters (unknown keys ignored, so additive backend drift is tolerated) but differs elsewhere: no @Json rename annotation (CodingKeys instead), stricter type-mismatch behavior, and default-value semantics that are expressed differently. ADR 0003's ContractSmokeTest and its field-presence coupling must be rebuilt in XCTest. :: REC: Option 2. Keep both decisions and their reasoning intact. From ADR 0002 preserve: the backend's tolerant-reader behavior (legacy-value migration before validation, loose-typed pass-through fields) IS the cross-cadence compatibility mechanism, and only TWO directions are established — older app → newer backend, and newer backend → older app; newer app → older backend is NOT proven and is handled defensively per ADR 0001. From ADR 0003 preserve the proportionality argument for a smoke test over a structural diff, and the pin-refresh procedure: copy the platform's openapi.json byte-for-byte and bump contract/CONTRACT_VERSION. Reject option 3 for the same reason Android did. Critically, carry the KNOWN BLIND SPOT forward unchanged, because Swift behaves identically here: removal of a nullable or defaulted consumed field decodes to nil silently and is NOT caught by round-trip parsing. The known instance — Nightscout pump_events[].units, where a null DROPS the event and UNDERSTATES IoB — must stay covered by explicit field-presence coupling against the pinned spec, and the rule stands that any new nullable consumed field whose absence would silently degrade a safety surface must be added to that coupling.
- README device verification table (authoritative safety status) :: The medical disclaimer defers to this table as the authoritative per-device safety status, so it cannot be inherited across platforms — an iOS BLE stack is a different code path than the Android one, and a 'Verified' claim carried over would be a false safety claim about software nobody has run. iOS verification also starts materially worse: the team has one Tandem t:slim X2 on one maintainer's iPhone, no Medtronic hardware, no Tandem Mobi, and no Simulator path for any BLE at all. :: REC: Option 1, and option 3 is not acceptable at any point. Add a column the Android table lacks — 'validated on iOS hardware by / on date' — and make a named tester plus a date the precondition for any status better than 'unverified'. Expected v1 state: Tandem t:slim X2 verified on iOS hardware by the named maintainer; Tandem Mobi protocol-compatible, unverified on hardware, no tester; Medtronic MiniMed 680G/770G/780G beta, read-only, unverified on hardware, no tester. Port unchanged alongside it: the pre-Overview IMPORTANT SAFETY WARNING blockquote, the `## Disclaimer` section (use at your own risk, not FDA-approved, does not control any medical device, AI can be wrong, verify dosing and carb-ratio and correction-factor suggestions with your care team), and the `## License` section — still the ONLY README section packaged into the app, so its no-later-`## `-heading constraint and its exact content (project name and description, Copyright (C) 2026 Josh Engelbrecht, GPL-3.0 with SPDX GPL-3.0-only and no or-later, pointers to LICENSE and the SPDX header policy, inbound=outbound, the GPL no-warranty paragraph) are all still test-enforced.
- PRIVACY.md — the outbound-connection and storage claims :: Three of the four documented outbound connections change and one disappears entirely. The Wear OS Data Layer over Google Play Services becomes WatchConnectivity, which removes a third party from the path. The GitHub release-API 'Check for updates' connection has no iOS install path behind it. EncryptedSharedPreferences and the SQLCipher-encrypted Room database become Keychain and Data Protection. And a user-built app has no App Store privacy label and no Apple review of any of these claims. :: REC: Option 1; option 2 implies a review that never happens. New claim set, keeping the same 'exactly N outbound connections, all user-chosen' structure: (1) the pump over BLE, direct and local — unchanged; (2) the self-hosted backend if configured, with the backend-optional local mode still fully functional (local BLE monitoring, on-device storage, local threshold alerts, zero network calls) — unchanged and load-bearing; (3) the watch over WatchConnectivity, an Apple-local transport carrying glucose, trend, IoB and alerts only, never credentials — and state explicitly that unlike Wear OS this involves no third-party services transport; (4) the GitHub release check becomes at most an explicitly user-initiated version comparison with no download, and Apple/TestFlight replaces it as the update mechanism, seeing the user's own Apple ID and install metadata under the user's OWN developer account. Storage claims: credentials in the Keychain with a device-only after-first-unlock accessibility class; glucose and insulin in an encrypted store under a file-protection class no weaker than complete-until-first-user-authentication; the watch keeping only a small rolling render cache in app-private storage, no credentials, and no network requests. Keep: no telemetry, no analytics, the platform repo's privacy page as canonical on conflict, and reporting through the SECURITY.md channels. Add the honest new sentence: these claims describe the source at a given tag; a user's own build is their own.
- MEDICAL-DISCLAIMER.md — warnings, distribution, forks, liability :: Two clauses become factually false and one becomes materially stricter. 'Project-owned unofficial builds are sideloaded from this repo's Releases page only; an APK from anywhere else is not covered' has no iOS analogue — there IS no project-owned build, and every binary is user-built. And Critical Warning #4's alert-delivery dependency list (BLE holding, Android scheduling background work, watch in range, phone charged) is Android-specific and understates iOS's constraints. :: REC: Option 2 — see questions_for_pm; the Android disclaimer already reserves 'users become the manufacturer of their own personal medical device' for fork users following the Loop/AndroidAPS precedent, and on iOS that description arguably fits every user. Warning #4's iOS list must be strictly MORE conservative and must never be weakened: alerts depend on the BLE link holding, iOS not having suspended or terminated the app, Core Bluetooth state restoration successfully relaunching it, Background App Refresh being enabled, Low Power Mode being off, the user's Focus / Do Not Disturb / notification-summary / per-app mute settings, and the watch being in range — and a user-built non-App-Store app cannot use the Critical Alerts entitlement, so notifications are silenceable by ordinary device settings. The supplement-not-replacement claim and the instruction to keep the pump's and CGM's own alerts enabled stay verbatim and become more load-bearing. Preserve unchanged: the five Critical Warnings' structure, the AI limitation list (hallucination, misinterpreting glucose data, outdated guidance, missing context), all AI content labelled suggestions not medical advice, the third-party-fork refusal, the untested-device clause (protocol compatibility does not guarantee correct operation; verify IoB, glucose and basal against the manufacturer's official app; the README table is authoritative), the caps-then-GPL-§15/16/17 liability grounding, the EU/UK/Australia jurisdictional note, and the statement that the PRIMARY risk mitigation is the monitoring-only design rather than the disclaimer.
- docs/dev/ section — the eight developer-reference pages :: Five of the eight document Android, Gradle and GitHub-Actions mechanics with materially different iOS answers; two need renaming to match the new architecture; one ports with new globs. The section shape, frontmatter contract and curated non-alphabetical order all survive — the contents do not. :: REC: Option 1; the section is how a maintainer reproduces every gate, and deferring it strands the CI subsystem. Page mapping: instrumented-tests-ci → XCUITest on Simulator destinations, PLUS an explicit statement of the hardware-test gap, since Core Bluetooth does not exist in the Simulator and no CI runner can exercise BLE. security-testing → the Swift gate stack with its own honest 'kept vs dropped vs newly-needed' table (kept: SAST, dependency CVE, secret scanning, workflow hardening; dropped: everything targeting a running server; newly needed: a Swift-capable SAST, since the Kotlin/Java packs cover nothing here). dependency-updates → Renovate's SwiftPM support over Package.swift and Package.resolved, with the same Tier-A/Tier-B auto-merge policy and the same note about why a lockfile-equivalent must be committed for the CVE gate to have anything to scan. release-and-promotion → the develop→main promotion, release-please versioning, changelog PR and sync-back all survive; what changes is that the release produces a TAG and no binary artifact. signing-and-secrets → per-fork Apple credentials, above. plugin-architecture → driver-architecture for compile-time SwiftPM targets. wear-os-architecture → watch-architecture (phone app plus watch app, WatchConnectivity, WidgetKit complications, Smart Stack). spdx-header-policy → ports with new globs. ADD one page the Android repo has no need for: fork-and-build.md, the maintainer-side reference for the per-user TestFlight pipeline, its secret contract, and its failure modes.

### SAFETY-CRITICAL (12)
- docs/contract/safety-constants.md is the single source of truth for three cross-repo constants whose desync is a patient-safety failure: mmol↔mg/dL factor 18.0156, glucose-validity bound 20..500 mg/dL, Tandem epoch offset 1199145600 s (2008-01-01 UTC). Canonical storage is mg/dL everywhere; mmol/L is display-only (÷ 18.0156, round last); alert/SSE numerics stay mg/dL. Any change must land in both repos in the same coordinated release and update the page plus SafetyConstantDriftGuardTest's enumeration in the same PR.
- The 20..500 bound has no single owner and is duplicated across ~17 shipped sites listed in safety-constants.md, including two documented off-by-one variants in AuthRepository.kt (20..499 and 21..500) and a SQL `BETWEEN 20 AND 500` in PumpDao.kt. A Swift port must consolidate rather than replicate the scatter, and must not lose the off-by-one sites silently.
- CONTRIBUTING mandates the plugin-side validation behavior: a reading outside SafetyLimits must be DISCARDED — not returned, not emitted, not persisted — and the rejection logged with the violated limit. This is a hard rule for any Swift driver.
- MEDICAL-DISCLAIMER Critical Warning #4 is an explicit alert-delivery non-guarantee: app alerts depend on the BLE connection holding, the OS scheduling background work, the watch being in range, and the phone being charged; they are a supplement to the pump's and CGM's own alerts, never a replacement, and device alerts must stay enabled. Any iOS port must carry this claim and must not strengthen it.
- No therapeutic write surface anywhere: no bolus dosing, no basal rate changes, no pump-setting modification, and no insulin delivery primitive on any capability interface. Permitted non-therapeutic device-management ops are exactly CGM calibration, BLE pair/unpair, and connect/disconnect. This is asserted independently in MEDICAL-DISCLAIMER.md, SECURITY.md, CONTRIBUTING.md, README.md, and the CodeRabbit Medical Safety gate; SECURITY.md pre-declines any report proposing the boundary be 'fixed'.
- ADR 0001's fail-open carve-out: monitoring/read paths fail open with a dismissible warning when backend compatibility is unknown, but a therapeutic or otherwise safety-critical write must be REFUSED under the same condition. If iOS ever adds such a surface it must not inherit the monitoring fail-open default.
- ADR 0003 blind spot: removal of a nullable/defaulted consumed field is not caught by round-trip parsing. The known instance is Nightscout pump_events[].units — a null DROPS the event and UNDERSTATES IoB. Covered only by field-presence coupling to the pinned spec. Any new nullable consumed field that degrades a safety surface when absent must be added to that coupling.
- SECURITY.md classifies incorrect BLE parsing that yields a wrong glucose, IoB, or dosing-history value as a security-channel report, not an ordinary bug — the private disclosure path must exist for wrong-number defects, not just classic vulnerabilities.
- CodeRabbit's hard-fail list encodes the concrete conversion hazards: storing/comparing an mmol/L value as mg/dL (persisting 3.9 instead of ~70 silently suppresses low alerts), converting the wrong direction, permitting a stored mg/dL value outside 20-500 after conversion, or using a factor other than 18.0156.
- Untested-hardware clause: Tandem Mobi is protocol-compatible but unverified on hardware, and Medtronic 680G/770G/780G is beta and unverified. Safety-critical values (IoB, glucose, basal) must always be verified against the manufacturer's official app. The README table is the authoritative per-device status the disclaimer defers to; an iOS port must publish its own equivalent table rather than inheriting this one.
- Medtronic doc records that SmartGuard auto-basal micro-boluses are not yet fully attributed on the on-device read path — a known incompleteness in insulin display.
- The in-app license viewer's Unavailable state exists only as a packaging failure signal; shipping an APK/IPA whose license section is blank is treated as a build-breaking defect (GradleException), not a runtime degradation.
